**Confirmed:**

- Overview.tsx riskIndex memo is at exactly lines 397-408 and matches the cited formula `avg*0.7 + criticalDensity*100*0.3` verbatim; `cases` is the windowed `api.listCases({limit:200, from:'now-${hours}h'})` sample (Overview.tsx:204), and `derived.critical` counts ALL statuses (not just open) via the LOCAL `bandOf` ladder (>=80 critical, Overview.tsx:149-156) — confirms the 'incoherent blend' characterization is accurate, including the LOCAL-ladder detail.
- widgets/risk.tsx:17-19 reads `data?.avg_risk_score` verbatim (RiskGaugeWidget) and registry.ts:254-256 has type 'gauge.active_risk' with description 'The mean case risk score as a gauge.' — both citations accurate.

- palette.ts scoreBand (line 224-229) is exactly 74/48/22 — matches plan's '74/48/22 ladder' claim; this is a DIFFERENT ladder from Overview's local bandOf (80/60/35/15, Overview.tsx:149-156) — the plan is correct that these diverge.

**CORRECTIONS:**
- Plan prose (PLAN_v1.md:9) lists 'new/open/investigating/escalated/on_hold/needs_human/reopened' as the non-terminal statuses. There is NO 'reopened' (or 'REOPENED') value in the CaseStatus enum anywhere in backend/app/constants.py (grep across constants.py + models.py returns zero hits); the real enum (constants.py:213-220) is exactly {NEW, OPEN, NEEDS_HUMAN, INVESTIGATING, ESCALATED, ON_HOLD, RESOLVED, CLOSED} — 6 non-terminal + 2 terminal. This is cosmetic (the code correctly uses `not in TERMINAL_CASE_STATUSES`, which is right), but the prose could send an implementer hunting for a status that doesn't exist. Note: webui Overview.tsx:164 DOES have a dead `t === 'reopened'` branch in a local `statusBar()` helper — that's pre-existing frontend dead code (related to item #11's scope), not a real backend status, and item #1 should not treat it as one.
- Plan text (PLAN_v1.md:17) says 'the CaseTriageHeader.tsx:154 comment already warns the copies must stay in sync' — the actual 'kept in sync with the backend copy' language is at CaseTriageHeader.tsx:152-153, not 154 (154 is the following clause about where it's shown/used as fallback). Minor citation drift, no functional impact.
- Plan's backend test-plan step ('backend/app/engine/metrics.py test: compute_metrics over [open risk 80, open risk 40, closed risk 10]...' in test_round3_wave2_metrics.py) is NOT directly writable with that file's existing `_case()` helper as-is: `_case()` (test_round3_wave2_metrics.py:45-78) hardcodes `risk_score=50.0` on every constructed Case with no `risk` kwarg (confirmed: only one `risk_score=` occurrence in the whole file, at line 77, and it is not parameterized). To build the plan's own worked example (differing per-case risk_score of 80/40/10) the implementer must first add a `risk: float = 50.0` parameter to `_case()` (mirroring the sibling helper in test_vigil_wave3.py:16, which already has `risk=50.0`) or construct `Case(...)`/`.model_copy(update=...)` directly — the plan's 'Add a test' bullet omits this prerequisite edit.
- Plan's test-plan step 2 ('assert active_risk_index/active_risk_case_count keys present in the /api/metrics router response — extend the existing metrics_client test') targets the WRONG fixture. The `metrics_client` fixture (test_round3_wave2_metrics.py:312-324, and the same-named fixture in test_round3_wave5_posture.py) mounts ONLY `app.api.routes_metrics.router`, which serves `/api/metrics/posture`, `/api/mitre/coverage`, `/api/mitre/coverage/navigator.layer.json` — it does NOT expose the plain `GET /api/metrics` route being modified (that route lives in `app/api/routes.py:1324` under the main `router`, mounted via the full-app `client` fixture, conftest.py:219). The correct existing tests to extend are `test_vigil_wave3.py::test_metrics_endpoint` (lines 121-125, `client.get('/api/metrics')`) and/or `test_metrics_disposition.py::test_metrics_endpoint_includes_by_disposition` (lines 35-38, same route). As written, following the plan literally would add assertions to a test that can never see the new keys.

**Risks:**
- Client-fallback ambiguity: Overview.tsx's existing local `OPEN_STATUSES` set (line 108: `{'open','investigating','in_progress','new','on_hold'}`) EXCLUDES 'needs_human' and 'escalated', which diverges from the backend's TERMINAL_CASE_STATUSES-based definition (only resolved/closed excluded — needs_human/escalated count as OPEN). The plan's 'client fallback over open cases in the loaded sample' doesn't specify which predicate to reuse; naively reusing OPEN_STATUSES (the obviously-available local set) would silently disagree with the canonical backend semantics the moment the fallback fires (e.g. minimal/legacy mocks, or the existing overview.render.test.tsx/overview.a11y.test.tsx fixtures which include a 'needs_human' case at risk_score=65 alongside an 'open' case at 88 — under OPEN_STATUSES only the 88 counts, under the canonical TERMINAL-based definition both 88 and 65 count, average 76.5 vs 88). Implementer should define a fresh predicate mirroring backend semantics (`status not in {'resolved','closed'}`), not reuse OPEN_STATUSES.
- The new ActiveRiskIndex.tsx / riskCopy.ts files will be scanned by the project's design gate (webui/scripts/gate-grep.mjs, run via `npm run gates` and design-gates.test.ts): 'no NEW arbitrary `text-[…]` size or raw `#rrggbb` hex in .tsx.' The plan doesn't call this constraint out; implementer must stick to the existing Tailwind text-size scale/tokens (as every other soc/components file does) or the gate fails.
- ARI is intentionally NOT window-scoped per the plan (open-pressure-now vs the TimeRangePicker's created-at window) — this is a genuine, disclosed behavior change from the old blend, but combined with the fact the KPI strip and severity widgets ARE window-scoped (derived from the same windowed `cases` array), the Command Center will show one number that ignores the selected time range sitting directly beside several that don't — worth a UX confirmation pass beyond just the tooltip disclosure.
- Removing the Row-A card changes Overview.tsx's Row-A DashboardGroup count from 3 to 2 — while no CURRENT test asserts on it, item #2/#3's own NEW test file (overview.commandcenter.test.tsx) and its updated overview.render.test.tsx assertions (PLAN_v1.md:88, 'assert the Active Risk Index badge renders in the header... drop the removed Active Risk Index/Cost & budget expectations') depend on item #1 (or #2/#3, whichever lands) having already made this exact edit — if items land out of the planned order the intermediate state could fail either item's new tests transiently.

**CORRECTIONS:**
- theme.css:198 / plan line 198 claim 'a grep confirms nothing references [--motion-*]' is not fully accurate (and it repeats an inaccuracy already present in TESTING_BRANCH_STATE.md line 1211). `webui/src/soc/dashboard/rgl-theme.css` (the react-grid-layout theme override for the custom-dashboard edit-mode grid) already consumes `var(--motion-base, 200ms)` / `var(--motion-fast, 120ms)` directly at lines 15-17 and 34 (with hardcoded ms fallbacks). It's a minor point -- that file isn't Tailwind-driven and isn't in the plan's edit list -- but the blanket 'nothing references them' claim should read 'nothing references them via Tailwind's transitionDuration/transitionTimingFunction config; one hand-authored CSS file (rgl-theme.css) already consumes the vars directly with literal fallbacks.'
- The plan's useCountUp spec (line 200, 209) says it 'returns target immediately when usePrefersReducedMotion() is true or matchMedia absent' -- but usePrefersReducedMotion()/useMediaQuery.ts (webui/src/soc/hooks/useMediaQuery.ts:44, 'Server / no-matchMedia: default to the not-matching snapshot') is explicitly documented to return `false` (i.e. NOT reduced, animate-by-default) when matchMedia is absent -- 'matching the app's current behaviour' per its own docstring. So relying on `usePrefersReducedMotion()===true` alone will NOT catch the matchMedia-absent case; the plan's parenthetical implies useCountUp must add its own independent `typeof window.matchMedia !== 'function'` check rather than delegating both conditions to the existing hook, which is a real design detail (not just wording) the plan glosses over and that contradicts the documented convention of the very hook it says it reuses. Should be called out explicitly in the useCountUp.ts file-touch bullet so the implementer doesn't assume the hook alone covers it.

This is a genuine double-booking of the same file/same lines, not just a shared-file touch -- whoever lands second will either produce a git conflict or silently duplicate the config block. Must be reconciled: only ONE of #5/#12 should add these keys (recommend #5 owns it since #5 is the actual consumer of duration-fast/base/slow via CountUp/Reveal/scale-in; #12 should drop its own bullet (2) and 'Files to touch' tailwind.config.js line entirely once #5 lands, or vice versa if #12 lands first).

Create only if #5 assigns count-up here' (plan line 683). Item #12's own text already defers correctly, but its 'Files to touch' / 'New contracts' sections (lines 683-684, 689-690) still list it as something #12 might build with a DIFFERENT prop shape (KpiTile `countUp?:boolean` vs #5's `countTo?:number`+`format?`+`animateValue?:boolean`) -- the orchestrator must explicitly kill #12's version once #5 lands, not just rely on #12's own 'only if' hedge, or both a useCountUp.ts AND a competing KpiTile prop convention could land.

Item #4's own plan (line 167) already flags '#3/#5/#8/#12 may also touch KpiTile' -- confirming this is a known, acknowledged hot spot, but it needs a single integrating reviewer/sequencer as the plan says, since interface-block insertions from #4 and #5 will likely land in the same few trailing lines (~60-63) of the props interface.

**Risks:**
- The useCountUp 'matchMedia absent -> snap' behavior (see corrections) needs an explicit independent guard inside useCountUp.ts rather than assuming usePrefersReducedMotion() covers it, since that hook is documented to return false (animate) when matchMedia is absent.

- tailwind.config.js double-booking (#5 vs #12) on the exact same transitionDuration/transitionTimingFunction keys must be resolved before either lands, or CI will show a spurious diff/conflict even though the JS object would still technically evaluate fine with duplicate-add attempts (first agent's edit will make the second agent's 'add these keys' instruction a no-op find-fails-to-match Edit error, since the exact old_string the second agent expects will no longer exist post-#5).
- No backend changes in this item, so guardrails #3 (decide() byte-identical) and route_auth_coverage (require_permission) are not applicable/at-risk here -- confirmed by design (plan explicitly states 'no API change', 'Backend pytest: unaffected').

Item #1's own plan already flags this as an unresolved 'Open question' ('if item #6 wants the hero shorter, fall back to the numeric-only variant') but neither plan commits to the fallback — this needs to be resolved as a hard sequencing decision, not left as a soft coordination note in #6's Shared Deps section.

**Risks:**

No current test asserts on it (so it won't show as a 'breaking test'), and it's unlikely to trip the jest-axe smoke test (axe doesn't mandate grouping roles), but it is a real, silent a11y regression the plan doesn't call out — worth an explicit note to either wrap the moved controls in an equivalent `<div role="group" aria-label="Dashboard controls">` or reuse a `variant="plain"` ControlBar shell inside `actions`.
- No pixel/layout test exists (nor is one proposed beyond class-presence checks) to verify the actual rendered height lands anywhere near the plan's claimed ~64-68px — this can only be confirmed by a manual/visual check (e.g. via the `run`/`verify` skill) once implemented, especially for Overview and Standup which both keep eyebrow+description and, in Standup's case, a nested two-row actions stack.
- The AutomationNudge slim-down is explicitly shared with item #12/beginner-journey per #6's own Shared Deps note; if #12's or the beginner-journey agent's copy differs from what #6 implements, whichever lands second will silently overwrite the other's banner text/markup — needs one owner for the final AutomationNudge.tsx content, not two independent edits.
- The Overview.tsx top-of-file architecture comment (lines 1-20) and the '~64px' inline comments (lines 8, 615) are not in the plan's Overview.tsx file-to-touch list; if left unedited, they will describe a CONTROL BAR zone that no longer exists as a separate band after this item lands, actively misleading the next agent who reads the file.

Item #7 exists only as a one-line DECISIONS.md table row plus a single cross-reference sentence inside item #6's 'Shared deps' (PLAN_v1.md:301). There is no committed spec for HOW to widen it (target width/breakpoints, whether it becomes a real persistent <input> vs. a wider dialog-opening Button, mobile/below-sm behavior, or whether CommandPalette.tsx itself changes). This is a planning gap, not a code inaccuracy — the orchestrator should have a real spec written (or explicitly scope it to 'widen the existing Button, keep it a dialog-opener') before assigning implementation.

**Exact edit points:**
- webui/src/soc/AppShell.tsx:544-556 — the <Button> element to widen; keep onClick={() => setPaletteOpen(true)}, aria-label="Open command palette", the CommandIcon + 'Search' text + <kbd>⌘K</kbd> children unchanged to avoid any behavior regression; only change size/className (e.g. explicit width classes replacing pure content-sizing, and/or reconsider the 'hidden ... sm:inline-flex' visibility gate if mobile discoverability is also in scope).
- webui/src/soc/AppShell.tsx:542 — <div className="ml-auto flex items-center gap-2"> is the right-cluster flex scope; this (or a new wrapper solely around the Button) is where any 'grow to fill available space' layout (e.g. flex-1 on the button, or reordering) must be scoped — do not touch the outer header's h-14/flex classes at line 531, since all right-cluster children are h-8 and fit h-14 regardless of width.
- webui/src/soc/AppShell.tsx:534-540 — the breadcrumb <nav>; already has min-w-0 (534) + truncate (539) on the page-label span — this is the ONLY existing shrink valve if a wider search consumes more of the row; verify it still reads at sm/md breakpoints after the change.
- If (and only if) the true intent is a persistent inline text input rather than a wider dialog-opening button: CommandPalette.tsx:237-243 (the <CommandInput>) is the sole existing query field, currently only reachable inside the Dialog/DialogContent (line 226-229) opened by the AppShell button — relocating it into the always-visible header is a materially larger restructuring of CommandPalette's props/mount contract and should be scoped/planned separately, not bundled into a 'widen the button' task.

**Breaking tests:**
- None, if the change is limited to widening/restyling the existing Button (AppShell.tsx:544-556) while keeping its aria-label, onClick, and the unchanged CommandPalette mount at AppShell.tsx:657-661 — verified no test in webui/src references the button's classes, label text, or a header snapshot.
- webui/src/soc/__tests__/command-palette.test.tsx (5 tests) and webui/src/soc/__tests__/registry-router-round6.test.tsx (2 CommandPalette tests) both render <CommandPalette open onOpenChange={...} onNavigate={...} /> standalone (not via AppShell) and assert on screen.getByPlaceholderText(/jump to a page, search cases\/sources/i) plus [cmdk-item]/[cmdk-group-heading] selectors (e.g. data-value="nav-cases", "navc-metrics-cost", "set-general") — these WOULD break only if the implementation restructures CommandPalette's internals/props (e.g. moving CommandInput out of the Dialog into the header, changing the open/onOpenChange contract), not from a pure AppShell-button width change.
- webui/src/soc/__tests__/bundle-first-paint.test.ts asserts CommandPalette is code-split out of the entry chunk (lazy-loaded) — re-run if any restructuring changes CommandPalette's import graph or how/where it's mounted.

**Conflicts:**
- None at the file level for the 'widen the existing Button' reading: AppShell.tsx (~527-633) does not appear in any other Round-7 item's committed 'Files to touch' list — checked #1 (Overview.tsx, riskCopy.ts, CaseTriageHeader.tsx, ActiveRiskIndex.tsx, dashboard/widgets/risk.tsx, dashboard/registry.ts), #6 (PageHeader.tsx, Overview.tsx, Standup.tsx, Dashboards.tsx, AutomationNudge.tsx, HeroPanel.tsx), and #5 (tailwind.config.js, theme.css, soc/motion.ts, hooks/useCountUp.ts, hooks/useInView.ts, components/CountUp.tsx, components/Reveal.tsx, KpiTile.tsx, RiskGauge.tsx, ui/tabs.tsx) — none touch AppShell.tsx.
- Latent/soft conflict: item #12's prose (PLAN_v1.md:671, an 'adoption map' narrative, NOT in its own committed Files-to-touch list at 676-685) floats promoting LoadingBar 'to a global route-progress affordance in AppShell', and item #5's own Open Questions (PLAN_v1.md:266) explicitly defers that same idea to 'the shell/#12 item'. Neither item currently owns AppShell.tsx as a committed file, but if a future wave adds a top-bar route-progress bar it would likely land at the top of the same <GlassSurface as="header"> (527-633) item #7 edits — not a live conflict today, but flag for a re-check at integration/sequencing time.
- Item #6 (PLAN_v1.md:301) already self-declares 'different region... no file overlap' with #7 — confirmed accurate by direct file-list comparison; the only shared concept is the word 'header' (item #6 owns the per-page PageHeader component; item #7 owns AppShell's global top bar — two separate React trees in two separate files).

**Risks:**
- No automated layout/overflow/visual-regression coverage exists for the AppShell top bar (vitest+RTL/jsdom only, which never computes real CSS box widths) — a widened Search element risks visually overflowing or squeezing NotificationBell/theme-toggle/version-badge/health-pill/UserMenu at the sm-md viewport range (640-1024px), and nothing in the test suite would catch it; recommend manual/visual verification (or a new Playwright check in a deploy-target session) before shipping.
- The header (AppShell.tsx:531) has no flex-wrap/overflow-x-auto/min-w-0 of its own; the only existing shrink valve is the breadcrumb's truncate (534-540). Widening the Button without gating the extra width behind a higher breakpoint (e.g. md:/lg: instead of sm:) or capping its own max-width risks squeezing the breadcrumb unreadable or pushing right-cluster items off-screen on narrower desktop widths.
- The Search affordance is entirely `hidden` below the `sm` breakpoint (className 'hidden ... sm:inline-flex') — there is currently zero visual search entry point on mobile/narrow viewports (only the Cmd+K keyboard shortcut, which is useless on touch). If 'expand horizontally' is also meant to improve mobile discoverability, that requires an explicit, separate design decision the current plan doesn't address.
- Because item #7 has no committed spec (see corrections), there is real risk an implementer over-scopes it into converting the button into a true persistent <input> (bypassing the Dialog), which would touch CommandPalette.tsx's mount/props contract and could break the tests listed above; recommend the orchestrator lock a concrete spec (wider dialog-opening Button vs. persistent inline input) before assigning implementation.

## [item] #8 Cases list polish + severity-columns fix (Cases.tsx, badges.tsx, DataTable.tsx, ColumnsMenu)


**Confirmed:**
- Root-cause bug is real and exactly as described: aiSeverity() (webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:150-152) returns c.risk_score verbatim; caseSeverity() (:155-159) reads a non-existent (c as Record).severity then also falls back to c.risk_score — so the 'Severity' column (:884-889) and 'Severity (AI)' column (:890-903) render SeverityBadge(risk_score) twice, pixel-identical.
- SortId union 'severity_ai' member is exactly at Cases.tsx:369 and its comparator at :384 (in the Record<SortId,...> literal spanning 373-388) — matches plan line citations exactly.
- Column count is exactly 17 today (confirmed by grepping every `id: '...'` column literal 791-963): case_id, title, status, disposition, alerts, playbooks, enrichments, category, severity, severity_ai, confidence, verdict, risk, urgency, entity, updated_at, actions.
- No test anywhere in the repo (full grep of webui/src and backend) references 'severity_ai' / aiSeverity / 'Severity (AI)' outside Cases.tsx itself — safe to delete.
- DataTable's `rowAccent` prop exists (DataTable.tsx:162) and is confirmed used/imported NOWHERE else in the codebase (grep -rl 'rowAccent' returns only DataTable.tsx) — 'currently unused anywhere' is accurate.
- usePrefs().tableColumns(tableId) returns `prefs.tables[tableId]` (prefs.tsx:219-222) which is `undefined` when unset — confirmed. Cases.tsx ALREADY computes `const columnState = tableColumns(CASES_TABLE_ID) ?? {};` at line 507 (this, not a new variable, is the real edit point for the curated default).
- ColumnsMenu wiring at Cases.tsx:1234-1238 (`<ColumnsMenu columns={columnMenuItems} state={columnState} onChange={handleColumnState}/>`) and DataTable at :1275-1316 with `columnState={columnState}` at :1278 match the plan's line citations exactly. `columnMenuItems` (line 989) is derived by mapping `columns`, so deleting the severity_ai column object automatically removes it from the menu with no extra work.
- SeverityBadge (badges.tsx:159-182) and its shared severityBand()/severityBandFromNumber() (badges.tsx:83-117) correctly accept either a raw 0-100 number or a band-name string and normalize via the ONE scoreBand ladder — compatible with the plan's proposed caseSeverityBand()/SeverityBadge usage.
- ProvenanceTag does not exist anywhere in the repo yet (grep -rn 'ProvenanceTag' returns nothing) — genuinely new, correctly flagged as shared with #9b.
- FilterBarGroup already exists and is exported from webui/src/soc/components/FilterBar.tsx (function FilterBarGroup at line 71) but Cases.tsx does not currently import it — available to reuse as the plan intends.
- All 5 existing Cases test files (cases-controls, cases-bulk, cases-bulk-tag-assign, cases-rbac, cases.a11y) mock `prefs.effective` with `tables: {}`, so tableColumns('cases') resolves to undefined in every one of them — the new CASES_DEFAULT_HIDDEN default will silently activate across ALL of them (not just the new test). Verified none of them assert on disposition/alerts/playbooks/enrichments/category/urgency column content, so this is currently non-breaking, but it's a repo-wide side effect the plan doesn't call out.
- backend/app/api/routes.py list_cases (decorator :2766, body 2767-2791) and get_case (decorator :2794, body 2795-2803) both already carry `Depends(require_permission('cases','read'))` — the guardrail is satisfied; item #8 introduces no new endpoints.

**CORRECTIONS:**
- CRITICAL — the plan's exact wiring `rowAccent={(c)=>caseSeverity(c)}` will NOT draw a severity-graded accent as claimed. caseSeverity(c) returns a raw risk_score number (0-100) in virtually all cases (no `severity`/`severity_band` field is ever populated pre-#9b). DataTable's rowAccent implementation (DataTable.tsx:463-467) passes that value straight to `semanticColor(String(accent))` (palette.ts:262-267) WITHOUT first banding it via scoreBand()/severityBand(). semanticColor() only recognizes band-name keys ('critical'/'high'/'medium'/'low'/'info', plus status/verdict names); a numeric string like '72' matches none of them and falls through to `categorical(0)` — the exact SAME fixed color on every single call (fallbackIndex defaults to 0 every time). Net effect: EVERY row would get an identical accent bar, the opposite of 'at-a-glance risk scanning'. This is a pre-existing latent defect in DataTable.tsx (its own JSDoc at lines 152-160 falsely promises a numeric score is 'resolved via the ONE palette authority') that has never been exercised because rowAccent has zero callers/tests today. Fix: wire `rowAccent={(c) => severityBand(caseSeverity(c)) ?? undefined}` (severityBand is already imported in Cases.tsx) instead of the raw value, and/or fix DataTable.tsx to call scoreBand() on numeric accents before semanticColor().
- CRITICAL — the plan's proposed `severity_source` shape for webui/src/lib/types.ts (`{ band: string; value: number; source: 'source_asserted'|'derived'; scale?: string } | null`) and the corresponding Cases.tsx rewrite (`c.severity_source?.value ?? ...`, `severityProvenance(c)=c.severity_source?.source==='source_asserted'?'source':'code'`) is WRONG and contradicts the REAL backend model. `backend/app/models.py:1176-1180` already declares severity_band/severity_source/impact_band/urgency_band/priority_level as FIVE SEPARATE FLAT `str | None` FIELDS (severity_source is literally `str | None  # "source_asserted" | "derived"`), not a nested object. This directly conflicts with item #9b's plan in the SAME document, which correctly proposes flat fields matching the real model, and which item #8 says it depends on/consumes. If item #8's code sketch were implemented verbatim it would never populate (c.severity_source is always a plain string on the wire, so `.value`/`.source` are undefined), silently degrading to the risk_score/'code' fallback forever — quietly defeating the entire provenance feature. Item #8 must adopt #9b's flat-string contract: `caseSeverityBand(c) = c.severity_band ?? <derive from risk_score>`; `severityProvenance(c) = c.severity_source === 'source_asserted' ? 'source' : 'code'`.
- The plan's models.py 'Files to touch' bullet under item #8 ('Add an additive optional field to class Case (:1104): severity_source: dict | None = None') is factually wrong on two counts: (1) it doesn't realize severity_band/severity_source/impact_band/urgency_band/priority_level ALREADY EXIST on Case at models.py:1176-1180 (item #9b's own plan correctly notes this) — nothing needs to be 'added'; (2) even if something were being added, the existing field type is `str | None`, not `dict | None`. This bullet should be dropped/corrected in favor of #9b's actual plan (a new `priority.advisory_bands()` helper + fail-open enrichment in routes.py — the field itself needs no schema change).
- Minor path error: the plan's 'Files to touch' list cites existing regression tests at 'webui/src/soc/pages/__tests__ (existing) — cases-severity-drill.round6.test.tsx, cases-controls, cases-bulk*, cases.a11y' — this directory is wrong. All of these files (and every Cases/Overview/Metrics page test) actually live in `webui/src/soc/__tests__/` (confirmed: `webui/src/soc/pages/__tests__/` contains a disjoint set — Approvals, AutomationNudge, Baseline, BatchJobs, Campaigns, casedetail/*, settings/*, etc. — no cases-*/overview/metrics files). The new test file `cases-severity-columns.test.tsx` is correctly placed at `webui/src/soc/__tests__/` per the plan, but the 'existing tests' bullet's directory is stale/wrong. (Item #9b's plan has the same directory error for its own new Cases.provenance.test.tsx — should also be webui/src/soc/__tests__/.)

**Exact edit points:**
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:150-152 — delete aiSeverity() helper.
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:155-159 — rewrite caseSeverity() (or rename per #9b reconciliation) to prefer the flat `c.severity_band` string over the dead `(c as Record).severity` read, falling back to risk_score.
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:362-371 — remove 'severity_ai' from the SortId union (currently at line 369).
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:373-388 — remove the `severity_ai:` comparator entry (currently line 384).
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:507 — THE actual first-run-default edit point: change `const columnState = tableColumns(CASES_TABLE_ID) ?? {};` to `... ?? { hidden: [...CASES_DEFAULT_HIDDEN] }` (plan describes this via an invented `effectiveColumnState` name; there is no such existing variable — line 507 is the one to change).
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:884-889 — Severity column cell; add ProvenanceTag using the CORRECTED flat-field severityProvenance(c).
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:890-903 — delete the entire severity_ai column object.
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:1234-1238 — ColumnsMenu `state={columnState}` (unchanged reference, benefits automatically once :507 is fixed).
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:1275-1316 — DataTable mount; add `rowAccent={(c) => severityBand(caseSeverity(c)) ?? undefined}` (NOT the plan's raw `caseSeverity(c)`) — severityBand is already imported from '@/soc/components/badges' at line ~87.
- webui/src/lib/types.ts ~1418-1488 (export interface Case) — add FLAT optional fields `severity_band?: string | null; severity_source?: string | null; impact_band?: string | null; urgency_band?: string | null; priority_level?: string | null;` (matching models.py:1176-1180), NOT the nested-object shape item #8 originally proposed.
- webui/src/soc/components/DataTable.tsx:463-467 — optional root-cause fix: call `scoreBand()` when `accent` is a number before `semanticColor()`, so the JSDoc promise (line 152-160) becomes true for ANY future numeric rowAccent caller, not just Cases.tsx's corrected call site.

**Breaking tests:**
- None of the 6 currently-green Cases tests (cases-controls.test.tsx, cases-bulk.test.tsx, cases-bulk-tag-assign.test.tsx, cases-rbac.test.tsx, cases-severity-drill.round6.test.tsx, cases.a11y.test.tsx) reference severity_ai/aiSeverity/'Severity (AI)' or any of the columns proposed for the new default-hidden set (disposition/alerts/playbooks/enrichments/category/urgency) — verified via full-text grep, so none literally break, but ALL of them will silently pick up the new CASES_DEFAULT_HIDDEN default (since they all mock tables: {}) the first time this lands; re-run all 6 after the change to be sure no incidental assertion (e.g. row/column counts) is order- or count-sensitive.
- cases-severity-drill.round6.test.tsx must be re-verified once caseSeverity() is rewritten to prefer c.severity_band: its 3 fixtures never set severity_band (undefined), so it must still fall back to risk_score (90/60/10 -> critical/high/low) for the test to keep passing — the plan's own note on this is correct, contingent on the fallback being implemented (not the broken nested-object version).

**Conflicts:**
- webui/src/lib/types.ts Case interface (~1418-1488): item #8 and item #9b both add `severity_source` (and friends) here with INCOMPATIBLE shapes (nested object vs. flat string) — must be reconciled to #9b's flat version before either lands (see corrections).
- webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx lines ~149-159 (severity helpers) and ~883-930 (severity/severity_ai/risk/urgency columns): both #8 and #9b independently rewrite this exact block — #8's caseSeverity()/severityProvenance() sketch and #9b's caseSeverityBand()/caseSeveritySource() sketch are different names/shapes over the same lines and must be merged into one function, not landed twice.
- backend/app/models.py Case class (~1104-1180): #8's plan incorrectly proposes 'adding' severity_source as a new dict field here; #9b's plan (correctly) treats it as pre-existing and only adds a new `advisory_bands()` helper in priority.py + routes.py wiring — implement per #9b, discard #8's models.py bullet entirely to avoid a duplicate/conflicting field redefinition.
- backend/app/api/routes.py list_cases/get_case (~2766-2803): both #8 and #9b flag the same fail-open enrichment call as a 'shared_dep' — must be implemented exactly once (recommend #9b's advisory_bands(), per its plan) or the two items will double-patch the same lines.
- webui/src/soc/components/badges.tsx UrgencyPill: #9b adds a `band?: string` prop and #9b's Cases.tsx sketch changes the urgency column to `<UrgencyPill band={c.urgency_band} .../>`, while #8's plan leaves the urgency column (Cases.tsx:923-930) untouched AND puts 'urgency' in CASES_DEFAULT_HIDDEN — sequencing matters: #8's column-visibility change should not assume #9b's prop exists yet, and whoever lands #9b's UrgencyPill rewiring must do so against Cases.tsx's post-#8 line numbers, not the pre-#8 ones cited in either plan section.

**Risks:**
- The rowAccent uniform-color bug (see corrections) would ship a functionally broken feature that LOOKS like it works in a quick visual check (a colored bar renders) but conveys no actual severity signal — likely to slip past casual review since it fails silently (no console error, no test coverage) unless the fix (severityBand() wrapping) is applied at the Cases.tsx call site or the DataTable.tsx root cause.
- If the type-shape conflict (nested vs flat severity_source) is not caught before implementation, the Cases list 'source' provenance tag would NEVER appear (always fall back to 'code'/'derived') even after the backend correctly starts populating severity_source, and this would be very hard to notice in manual testing since 'code · derived' is still a plausible-looking, honest-sounding label — a silent feature failure, not a crash.
- Curated first-run column hiding (disposition/alerts/playbooks/enrichments/category/urgency) depends entirely on tableColumns(CASES_TABLE_ID) being exactly `undefined` (not `{}` or `{hidden: []}`) to distinguish 'never customized' from 'user explicitly restored all columns' — Cases.tsx:507's current fallback is `?? {}`, so the plan is right that `??` is the correct operator (undefined only), but this is fragile if any other code path (e.g. a future 'reset columns' button) ever persists an explicit `{}` instead of clearing the key — worth a defensive comment.
- The optional backend severity_source/severity_band population is fail-open per both plans, but if it lands with the WRONG (dict) shape from #8's stale bullet instead of the correct (string) shape already defined in models.py, a schema mismatch would only surface as a Pydantic validation error at response-serialization time for existing cases with a manually-set severity_source — low likelihood since nothing sets it today, but worth flagging given the plan text explicitly describes redefining an already-typed field.

The plan's files-to-touch bullet for OverviewPanel.tsx does not list a new prop; the simplest correct fix is a NEW callback threaded down from CaseDetail.tsx (e.g. `onOpenTab: (tab: 'threat') => void`, trivially `() => setTab('threat')`), not reuse of `onNavigate`.

**Confirmed:**
- severity_band IS a dead field today: repo-wide grep for `severity_band\s*=`/`severity_source\s*=`/`impact_band\s*=`/`urgency_band\s*=`/`priority_level\s*=` across backend/app finds ZERO writers. The only production reader is `backend/app/api/routes_triage.py:535` (`StageState(severity_band=case.severity_band, severity_source=case.severity_source)` inside `_build_stages`), which therefore always reads None. Real case creation (`backend/app/agents/pipeline.py:511-532`) and the demo generator (`backend/app/engine/demo_generator.py:627-660`) both construct `Case(...)` without ever setting these 5 fields.
- models.py fields exist exactly as cited: `severity_band`/`severity_source`/`impact_band`/`urgency_band`/`priority_level` on class Case at backend/app/models.py:1176-1180, all `str|None = None`, with a doc comment (1171-1175) explicitly noting they are presentation-only and never read by decide().
- priority.py current cuts confirmed: `_BAND_HIGH_CUT = 70.0` / `_BAND_MEDIUM_CUT = 40.0` at backend/app/engine/priority.py:46-47 (3-band high/medium/low via `_band_from_magnitude`, line 54-58) — matches the plan's '70/40' claim exactly. `severity_band_from_events` (143-184), `impact_band` (187-202), `urgency_band` (205-218), `derive_priority` (221-258) and `derive_triage` (261-334) all exist with the signatures the plan describes; `advisory_bands()` does NOT yet exist (confirmed absent) so the plan correctly proposes adding it.
- routes.py list_cases (decorator :2766, `async def` at 2767-2791) and get_case (decorator :2794, `async def` at 2795-2803) exist as described; BOTH already carry `Depends(require_permission("cases","read"))` — since #9b adds no new endpoint (only enriches these two existing, already-authed routes), the route_auth_coverage guardrail is trivially satisfied.
- Populating severity_band/etc. on GET /api/cases and GET /api/cases/{id} is additive and safe: `CaseListResponse` (routes.py:2727-2732) wraps `cases: list[Case]`; `get_case` uses `response_model=Case`; the TS `Case` interface (webui/src/lib/types.ts:1418-1488) has a catch-all `[key: string]: unknown` index signature. Grepped backend/tests broadly (test_api.py, and all files matching case JSON assertions) — no test does exact-dict/closed-key-set equality on a Case or CaseListResponse payload; `test_poll_and_cases` (test_api.py:58-62) only checks `"cases" in r.json() and "total" in r.json()`. New optional fields cannot break any existing pytest.
- Cross-reference confirmed: badges.tsx `computeUrgency()` (webui/src/soc/components/badges.tsx:523-557) is a 4-band ladder (critical/high/medium/low) driven by risk_score + an age-since-created_at bucket (+25/+15/+5h) + escalation, genuinely divergent from the backend `urgency_band()` 3-band (high/medium/low) risk+escalation-only formula — exactly as the plan states. `UrgencyPill` (566-590) has no `band` prop yet, so the plan's additive prop is a clean, non-breaking addition. `URGENCY_VARIANT`/label map already includes a critical->'Urgent' back-compat mapping the plan reuses.

The decide-invariance test at line 230 (`test_decide_is_invariant_to_priority`) does not depend on specific cut values and stays green regardless.
- palette.ts `scoreBand` cuts confirmed exactly 74/48/22 at webui/src/soc/components/palette.ts:224-229 (`ScoreBand = 'low'|'medium'|'high'|'critical'`, 4-band, SCORE_BANDS documented 0-21/22-47/48-73/74-100).

**CORRECTIONS:**
- CROSS-ITEM CONFLICT (models.py + routes.py + types.ts, same field name, incompatible shapes): Item #8 ('Cases list — make it appealing', PLAN_v1.md lines 348-349) proposes 'backend/app/models.py — Add an additive optional field to class Case (:1104): severity_source: dict | None = None (or a small typed submodel {band,value,source,scale})' and a matching webui type 'severity_source?: { band: string; value: number; source: ...; scale?: string } | null' (line 347). But `severity_source` ALREADY EXISTS on Case as `str | None` (models.py:1177), already consumed as a plain string at routes_triage.py:535 via `StageState.severity_source: str|None`. Item #9b (this item, lines 471-474) correctly recognizes the EXISTING string-typed fields and writes to them via a new `advisory_bands()` helper, typing `severity_source` as `'source_asserted'|'derived'|string` in types.ts. If item #8 lands its own models.py/types.ts edit as literally written, it either (a) silently redefines the field with an incompatible type (dict vs str), breaking the existing `StageState(severity_source=case.severity_source)` call at routes_triage.py:535, or (b) collides with #9b's edit to the same field on the same line range. The orchestrator must force item #8 to adopt #9b's existing-string-field shape (`c.severity_band` + `c.severity_source: string`) rather than inventing a competing nested-object field — item #8's own Cases.tsx code samples (`c.severity_source?.value`, `c.severity_source?.source==='source_asserted'`) must be rewritten against the real (string) contract.

In reality `webui/src/soc/components/palette.ts`'s `scoreBand`/`ScoreBand` type is only 4-band (low/medium/high/critical, cuts 74/48/22 — no 'info', no 8-cut; palette.ts:221-237). - backend/app/models.py Case.severity_source (models.py:1176-1180): item #8 plans to 'add' this field as `dict|None` while it already exists (and #9b relies on it existing) as `str|None` — direct type conflict, must be resolved by the orchestrator before either lands (recommend: #8 defers to #9b's string-typed field).
- backend/app/api/routes.py list_cases (:2766-2791): both item #8 (line 348, a bespoke inline `severity_band_from_events(case, state.prefs)` call) and item #9b (line 472, the fuller `priority.advisory_bands(case, state.prefs)` helper covering 5 fields) propose editing the SAME function for overlapping purposes — only one implementation should land; #9b's is the superset and should own it, with #8 simply consuming the resulting fields.
- webui/src/lib/types.ts Case interface (~line 1418-1488): item #8 proposes `severity_source?: {band,value,source,scale}|null` (nested object) vs item #9b's `severity_band?/severity_source?/impact_band?/urgency_band?/priority_level?: string|null` (flat scalars) — same conflict as above surfacing in the frontend contract; webui code written against one shape will not compile/behave correctly against the other.
- webui/src/soc/pages/casedetail/StageTimeline.tsx:76-77 and webui/src/soc/pages/Cases.tsx:150-159/884-922: both item #9a (CaseDetail story-spine) and item #8 (Cases list) plan to consume the shared `ProvenanceTag`/`severityProvenance()` primitive that item #9b (this item) is scoped to deliver (webui/src/soc/components/ProvenanceTag.tsx) — sequencing dependency, not a line-range collision, but #9b must land its primitive + `FIELD_PROVENANCE` map before #8/#9a can consume it without shipping divergent local stand-ins.

**Risks:**
- The severity/impact/urgency cut-point unification (70/40 -> 74/48/22/8) is a real, non-cosmetic ADVISORY-display behavior change across every existing case (severity 74-100 flips High->Critical; urgency/impact 'high' cut drops 70->48, 'medium' cut drops 40->22) — confirmed via test analysis to break 4 additional test functions in test_round3_wave5_triage.py beyond the 3 lines the plan already accounts for in test_round3_wave2_triage.py. decide() itself stays byte-identical (verified: no code path feeds these bands into case_manager.decide()), so #3 holds, but the test-update surface is larger than scoped.
- Enrichment is wired ONLY at the HTTP-route layer (list_cases/get_case in routes.py), not at the store or case-creation layer, so several OTHER existing internal readers of the same fields stay unpopulated even after #9b ships: (a) `routes_triage.py:519-696` `case_stages`/`_build_stages` fetches its own fresh case via `state.cases.get(case_id)` at line 689 (bypassing routes.py's get_case enrichment), so `StageState.severity_band` (read at line 535) stays None and `StageTimeline.tsx`'s existing severity chip (guarded by `if (state.severity_band)` at StageTimeline.tsx:76) will continue to never render; (b) `engine/metrics.py::sla_metrics()` (lines 351-429, reading `c.priority_level` at 372/436) is fed cases via `routes_metrics.py:55`'s own direct `state.cases.list()` call, so the SLA breach/at-risk feature (config.py SlaPolicy, default enabled=False) would still silently score every case's `priority_level` as '' (excluding it from SLA evaluation) if ever enabled by an operator, even post-fix. Recommend either documenting this as an intentional, narrow scope limit (list/detail JSON display only) or moving the enrichment into a shared helper called by every Case-serving code path (ideally at the CaseStore.get()/list() layer) so the fields are genuinely non-dead everywhere they're read.
- Per-case `advisory_bands()` invocation inside list_cases adds deterministic CPU work for up to 200 rows per request (iterates `prefs.sources` per case inside `_scale_for_case`); negligible in practice but must stay wrapped in try/except so a single malformed case (missing entity/risk_breakdown) can never 500 the whole list — the plan already flags this, confirmed as a legitimate but manageable risk.
- Minor/cosmetic: the plan's own citation of 'TESTING_BRANCH_STATE §17' and 'palette.ts scoreBand' (for the info<8 cut) are both slightly wrong pointers (§18 and badges.tsx respectively) — low risk of misdirecting an implementer to the wrong reference file, no functional consequence since the underlying numbers are correct.

**CORRECTIONS:**
- CRITICAL — ★a and ★b (PLAN_v1.md lines 729-802 vs 804-869) specify TWO INCOMPATIBLE contracts for the same feature, both claimed to land in the SAME files (routes_metrics.py, engine/metrics.py). ★a: `GET /api/metrics/funnel?window_hours=24` → `{stages[], drops, reduction, counters, cases_meta}` built by NEW `engine/metrics.py::funnel_outcomes_by_severity(cases, prefs)` (line 748/765). ★b: `GET /api/metrics/noise-reduction?window_hours=24` → a differently-shaped `NoiseReduction{ingested,clustered,cases,outcomes,reduction_pct,human_reduction_pct,partial,counters_since,truncated,store_total}` built by a DIFFERENTLY-NAMED `engine/metrics.py::noise_reduction_metrics(cases, ingest_counters, window_hours, store_total)` (line 826-831). ★b even flags this as an open question ('confirm the counter work-item agrees, or align on /metrics/noise / /metrics/funnel', line 863) but ★a's text shows no awareness of ★b's divergent shape. One canonical endpoint path + response shape + engine-function name must be picked before implementation.
- Severity-ladder collision with item #9b (PLAN_v1.md lines 457-532, same file engine/priority.py). ★a proposes a brand-new constants.py `SEVERITY_BANDS` 5-band ladder 'aligned to OCSF_SEVERITY_TO_SCORE' (its own test-plan implies boundaries ~10/30/50/75/90) and re-bands via its own `funnel_band(value)` from the RAW magnitude `severity_band_from_events()` already computed — explicitly NOT trusting that function's own `band` field. Independently, #9b rewrites `engine/priority.py`'s cut constants to `_CRIT=74/_HIGH=48/_MED=22/_INFO=8` (matching frontend palette.ts scoreBand) and changes `severity_band_from_events()` itself to use that ladder (plan line 471). Net effect if both land as written: the SAME case's severity chip (post-#9b, 74/48/22/8 cuts) and the Noise-Reduction funnel's per-band outcome tally (★a's independent OCSF-derived cuts) would disagree — reproducing exactly the 'two conflicting severity ladders' bug both items claim to be fixing. Neither item's Shared-deps section names the other (★a names only 'Item #1'; #9b's shared-deps names #1/#3/#4/#5/#9/#11 but not ★a). Needs a single canonical ladder both engine/priority.py and engine/funnel.py import from constants.py.
- KV-pattern precedent is misattributed. The plan says FunnelCounterStore uses 'the exact zero-migration KV pattern of stores/baseline.py'. Verified false as stated: every existing zero-migration KV store (baseline.py:92-174, memory.py:95-141, dashboards.py:~99/119, case_thread.py:~76/91, user_prefs.py:~84/106) uses exactly ONE FIXED (namespace,key) pair for its whole dataset (confirmed via grep — each store's every kv_mutate/get/put call passes the same constant NS/KEY). FunnelCounterStore's proposed per-UTC-day DYNAMIC key ('funnel','YYYY-MM-DD') is a genuinely NEW usage pattern in this codebase, not a copy of any existing store's pattern — it is mechanically sound (see 'confirmed' above) but is architecturally closer to CursorStore.load_keyed/save_keyed (stores/cursor_store.py:41-54, which is ES-direct, not KVStore-based) than to baseline.py. This also undermines the plan's separate 'reset.py should call funnel_counters.clear()' claim (next item).
- engine/reset.py integration is underspecified/likely non-functional as described. ★a's Shared-deps says 'the cases/logs reset tier should call funnel_counters.clear() so a reset also drops the durable counters.' But the real reset.py (backend/app/engine/reset.py:114-141) clears KV stores via a hardcoded tuple of literal (namespace,key) pairs (`_CASES_KV`, e.g. `(BASELINE_NS, BASELINE_KEY)`), each wiped by ONE `_clear_kv(app_state, ns, key)` call — this only works because those stores use a single fixed key. A day-sharded FunnelCounterStore has no enumerable list of which day-keys exist (KVStore has no scan — the plan itself notes this elsewhere as a retention risk), so a bare `funnel_counters.clear()` has nothing to enumerate unless the store separately persists a 'known dates' index (not present anywhere in the plan's Files-to-touch/New-contracts for funnel_counters.py). This needs its own design step, not a one-line reset.py hook.
- Concrete crash risk in the proposed poller.py placement (HIGH severity). The plan says to compute `ingested_by_band`/`clusters_by_band` and invoke the funnel sink 'right before the existing audit write' in poll_once — i.e. after the `if new_events:` block ends (poller.py:360) and before `await self._audit.record(...)` (poller.py:372). But `clusters` (assigned at poller.py:342) and `cluster_stats` (assigned at poller.py:345) are LOCAL variables scoped ONLY inside `if new_events:` (lines 309-360); on any poll tick where `new_events` is empty (the common case — most polls find nothing new), these names are never bound. Referencing them unconditionally near line 372 raises UnboundLocalError on every quiet tick. `poll_once` itself has no internal try/except around this region (the only surrounding guard is in `_run()`'s outer `try: await self.poll_once(prefs) except Exception: logger.exception(...)`, poller.py:400-403), so such a crash would be caught at the loop level but would abort the ENTIRE poll_once call before it reaches the cursor-save loop (poller.py:366-370) and the audit-record call — silently breaking cursor persistence and case creation on every quiet cycle, not just funnel counting. The implementer must default `clusters = []` / `cluster_stats = {}` before line 309 (or otherwise branch on `new_events` truthiness) before adding any code that references them outside the existing conditional.
- severity_scale_for_source's proposed extraction range (priority.py lines 96-112) conflates two different responsibilities and misses an edge case. Lines 96-100 are the `for s in prefs.sources: find inst by id` LOOKUP loop (resolving a SourceInstance FROM a source_id string + prefs); lines 101-112 are the CLASSIFICATION logic that operates on an ALREADY-RESOLVED SourceInstance. Since the funnel call site (poller.py:340, `own_source = prefs.source_by_id(...)`) already holds a resolved SourceInstance, the natural DRY signature `severity_scale_for_source(src: SourceInstance|None)` should consume only lines 101-112 (no re-lookup) — the plan's cited '96-112' straddles both responsibilities and the implementer must pick one signature. Separately, `_scale_for_case`'s lines 94-95 special-case `source_id == 'demo'` → OCSF_0_100 BEFORE the lookup loop, precisely because the seeded demo connector's synthetic source_id ('demo', engine/demo_generator.py:42 DEMO_SOURCE_ID) is typically NOT a registered SourceInstance in prefs.sources. A `severity_scale_for_source(src)` called with an already-resolved (and here, None) src would silently fall to _SCALE_UNKNOWN instead of the correct OCSF_0_100 unless the demo-shortcut is preserved somewhere. Low practical risk in isolation — Demo Mode gates the real poller/ingest entirely (poller.py:391-399 skips poll_once when demo_active; demo telemetry flows through a separate DemoSimulator/store per that method's own docstring) — but the plan should state this explicitly rather than rely on it silently.
- Minor: plan says FunnelCounterStore writes go through 'base.kv_mutate (per-key lock + _rev CAS)'. If the implementer copies stores/baseline.py's literal pattern (ONE `self._lock = asyncio.Lock()` instance reused for every call regardless of which day-key is written, stores/baseline.py:94/113/135/154/162), the actual concurrency control is a single STORE-WIDE lock, not a genuine per-key lock (writes to today's and yesterday's day-doc would needlessly serialize against each other, though still correctly). A true per-key lock requires calling `KVStore.mutate()` (stores/base.py:280-291, which internally uses `self._lock_for(namespace,key)`) instead of hand-rolling one shared `asyncio.Lock()`. Not a correctness bug either way, just a mislabeling of the concurrency granularity the implementer should be aware of.

**CORRECTIONS:**
- ★b claims 'palette.ts is the single color authority — outcome-chip colors (success/critical/high/warning) and SEV_BAR tokens come from it; no local hex' and 'Reuses SEV token classes (bg-critical…bg-info)'. This is WRONG as stated: SEV_BAR (plus SEV_DOT/SEV_ORDER/SevKey) is a PRIVATE, non-exported module-level const defined inside webui/src/soc/pages/Overview.tsx:124-139, NOT in webui/src/soc/components/palette.ts. palette.ts (checked in full) exports SEVERITY_COLOR/STATUS_COLOR/VERDICT_COLOR/SEMANTIC_ICON/semanticIcon/scoreBand/SCORE_BANDS (palette.ts:146-235) but has NO severity→bg-class lookup map today. NoiseFunnel.tsx cannot 'reuse' SEV_BAR from palette.ts because it isn't there — the plan must either (a) define its own local {critical:'bg-critical',high:'bg-high',medium:'bg-medium',low:'bg-low',info:'bg-info'} map (duplicating Overview's private one, acceptable since the Tailwind class names themselves ARE real and already used across many files: KpiTile.tsx, BarList.tsx, CaseTriageHeader.tsx, etc.), or (b) add extracting SEV_BAR out of Overview.tsx into palette.ts as an explicit NEW file-touch (not an already-true fact) so both Overview.tsx and NoiseFunnel.tsx import one shared map.
- Cross-item naming/contract mismatch between ★b (this item) and item #2+3, both in the SAME plan document: item #2+3's 'Files to touch'/'New contracts' sections literally say it will mount '<NoiseReductionFunnel>' and consume a hook 'useNoiseReduction(hours)' plus endpoint 'GET /api/metrics/noise-reduction?window_hours='. But ★b's OWN 'Files to touch' section defines the component as `NoiseFunnel` (not `NoiseReductionFunnel`) in webui/src/soc/components/NoiseFunnel.tsx, with NO separate hook — data is fetched inline inside Overview's existing load()/Promise.allSettled via api.noiseReduction(hours), stored in a `noise` state + a `fallbackNoise` memo. If item #2+3 lands its Overview.tsx edit assuming a `<NoiseReductionFunnel>`/`useNoiseReduction` symbol that ★b never exports, the build breaks (tsc will fail on an undefined import). Must be reconciled to ONE component/hook name before either lands — recommend using ★b's actual names (NoiseFunnel, no separate hook) since that is the item that actually builds the file.
- Endpoint-path/contract mismatch between ★a (backend) and ★b/item#2+3 (frontend), all in the SAME plan document, for what is supposed to be ONE feature: ★a's own 'Files to touch'/'New contracts' define the route as `GET /api/metrics/funnel` (backend/app/api/routes_metrics.py) with client method `webui/src/lib/api.ts — getFunnel(windowHours=24) => request<FunnelResponse>('GET','metrics/funnel',...)` and response type `FunnelResponse`. ★b's own 'Files to touch'/'New contracts' (and item #2+3's) instead define `GET /api/metrics/noise-reduction?window_hours=24`, client method `api.noiseReduction(hours)`, and response type `NoiseReduction` (with a materially different field shape: ★a's FunnelResponse has `stages[]`/`bands`/`drops`/`reduction:{overall_pct,correlation_pct,triage_pct}`/`counters`/`cases_meta`, while ★b's NoiseReduction has `ingested`/`clustered`/`cases`/`outcomes`/`reduction_pct`/`human_reduction_pct`/`partial`/`counters_since`/`truncated`/`store_total`). These are two incompatible wire contracts for the same feature proposed within the same plan — this is the single highest-priority reconciliation needed before ★a or ★b is implemented; whichever ships first should be treated as canonical and the other item's section rewritten to match (recommend picking one route name, one client method name, and one response shape, then updating both sections).

- Overview.tsx lines ~695-740 (Stagger close → Zone-3 open → Row-A 'Active Risk Index' DashboardGroup): touched by item #1 (deletes the Row-A card, changes grid to xl:grid-cols-2), item #2+3 (repositions/removes the same Row-A card as part of its Z1-Z6 rewrite and inserts its OWN Zone-3 Noise Reduction band), and ★b (inserts the NoiseFunnel band at the exact same seam, 'directly under the KPI Stagger'). Three items landing in the same ~45-line window — must sequence #1 → #2+3 → ★b (or hand ★b the final post-#2+3 Overview.tsx) as the plan's own Shared-deps sections already partially acknowledge, but the three-way overlap is broader than any single item's write-up states.
- Overview.tsx lines ~330-358 (autonomy memo) and ~196-260 (load()/nudge effect): ★b's fallback-data construction reads `autonomy`/`posture.quality` (unchanged by ★b) but item #11 independently patches the `decision_by === 'auto'` bug inside this SAME memo (line 349) and item #2+3 also proposes fixing the identical predicate in its own Zone-5 rewrite — ★b doesn't edit this memo itself but its fallback logic's correctness depends on whichever fix lands here; confirm final field values (`autoClosed` count) after #11/#2+3 land before finalizing ★b's fallback-outcome math.
- webui/src/lib/types.ts ~1849-1872 (Metrics interface + surrounding new-interface region): item #1 edits fields INSIDE `Metrics` (adds `active_risk_index?`/`active_risk_case_count?`), ★b adds NEW sibling interfaces (`NoiseSeverityBreakdown`/`NoiseStage`/`NoiseOutcomes`/`NoiseReduction`) in the same neighborhood, and ★a ALSO proposes types in the same file for `FunnelResponse`/`FunnelStage` — a naming collision if both ★a's and ★b's response-type additions land without the contract-name reconciliation flagged in Corrections (two different type names for the same concept, or worse, one overwriting/duplicating the other).
- webui/src/lib/api.ts ~1011-1014 ('Metrics + feedback analytics' section): ★a proposes `getFunnel(...)` here and ★b proposes `noiseReduction(...)` here — same insertion point, two different method names for (nominally) the same call; must collapse to one before implementation.
- webui/src/soc/__tests__/overview.render.test.tsx and overview.a11y.test.tsx hoisted-mock blocks: shared write target for items #1, #2+3, and ★b simultaneously (see Breaking tests above) — the highest-traffic shared-file conflict in this cluster of items.

**Risks:**
- The plan's stated risk 'Adding a 5th Promise to Overview's load must stay typeof-guarded' is correct and necessary but under-specified: a literal reading of the 'Files to touch' bullet ('fetch api.noiseReduction(hours) inside the existing Promise.allSettled') could be implemented naively by an agent as a bare 5th array element, which throws synchronously against BOTH current test mocks (confirmed above to define only 4 keys) as well as any production api client missing the method during a rolling deploy where webui ships ahead of backend — the exact guard expression should be spelled out in the plan rather than left as a stated intent.
- Until the ★a/★b endpoint-name and response-shape mismatch (Corrections) is resolved, implementing ★b in isolation risks building a NoiseFunnel/NoiseReduction pipeline against a `/metrics/noise-reduction` route that ★a never creates (★a builds `/metrics/funnel`) — the graceful-degradation design (fail-open, 404-tolerant) will mask this as 'counters warming up' indefinitely rather than surfacing the real integration bug, so a manual cross-check against whatever ★a actually ships is essential before merging ★b.
- The SEV_BAR duplication/extraction decision (Corrections) affects whether NoiseFunnel.tsx and Overview.tsx's severity coloring can silently drift over time (two copies of the same critical/high/medium/low/info → bg-* map) — low severity but worth a one-line ticket to extract SEV_BAR to palette.ts as the plan's own stated philosophy ('palette.ts is the single color authority') actually intends, since today it is not.
- clip-path trapezoid rendering (called out by the plan itself as needing a real-browser /verify pass) is unverifiable via static grep/read; I did not attempt to render it — flagging only that this specific visual claim is unverified by this pass and should get its own /verify run in both themes as the plan already recommends.

**Risks:**
- Naming/signature mismatch: #11 exports `isAutoClosedByAI(status?: string|null, decisionBy?: string|null): boolean` (2-arg, in badges.tsx) while #9a's shared.tsx wants a `isAutoClosed(c: Case): boolean` (1-arg, Case-typed) helper and describes it as '(either owned by #11 and imported, or added to casedetail/shared.tsx and re-exported)'. These are different names AND different signatures — a direct re-export is not possible; #9a will need a thin adapter (`export const isAutoClosed = (c: Case) => isAutoClosedByAI(c.status, c.decision_by)`), which the plan should state explicitly rather than implying a pass-through.
- DecisionCard.tsx's declared props `{ c: Case, rationale: CaseRationale | null }` cannot render the 'matched AutoClosePolicy clause' it claims to show, because that data (`policy_clause`, `objection window` context beyond the plain `objection_window_expires_at` timestamp) lives only in the `timeline`/`TimelineResponse`/`DecisionPayload` payload (CaseDetail.api.ts), not in `Case` or `CaseRationale`. If not corrected before implementation, the built DecisionCard will silently omit the policy-clause facts it was scoped to show, or an implementer will improvise a prop-shape change late, causing InvestigationPanel/DecisionCard rework.
- If items land out of the recommended #9a-first order, transient red states are likely: e.g. if #11 or #9b land first and edit OverviewPanel.tsx's soon-to-be-deleted secondary badge row, #9a's subsequent full-file rewrite will either silently discard their edits (lost work) or hit textual merge conflicts depending on tooling (patch vs. full-file diff) — worth an explicit orchestrator note to land #9a's CaseDetail.tsx/casedetail/* restructuring BEFORE #9b's and #11's narrow edits to the same files, even though #9a is a bigger/slower item.
- Cross-referencing gap: #9a's plan explicitly cross-references #10 in some places (CaseDetail.tsx FeedbackTab removal 'coordinate #10') but its Risks section mis-cites #11 instead of itself for the CaseDetail.tsx/shared.tsx/ConfirmActionDialog.tsx three-file claim (see corrections) — this kind of self-referencing slip suggests the plan sections were drafted somewhat independently per item without a final cross-check pass; recommend the orchestrator do one more consistency pass specifically on the 'Shared deps'/'Risks' subsections before assigning work, since at least one is demonstrably wrong.
- #12 (outside my four assigned items) duplicates #9a's StageTimeline.tsx 114/184 fix without cross-referencing it, even though #12's OTHER CaseDetail.tsx-adjacent risk notes DO explicitly defer to '#9/#10' — an inconsistent cross-referencing discipline within the same document that could cause two independent agents to both 'fix' the same 2 lines, wasting an implementation cycle (low severity, but a real coordination gap the orchestrator should close by explicit assignment before work starts).

- backend/tests/test_route_auth_coverage.py::test_every_api_route_is_auth_covered and ::test_authz_exempt_allowlist_is_minimal_and_disjoint will NOT break for the new GET-only funnel/noise-reduction endpoint (GET routes don't need the non-GET AuthZ-exempt bucket, and require_permission('metrics','view') already satisfies the authN coverage walk) PROVIDED the implementer does not forget the require_permission dependency — omitting it would fail test_every_api_route_is_auth_covered immediately (a hard CI gate, not a silent regression) so this is self-catching, not a silent-break risk.
- backend/tests/test_round4_wave3_wiring.py::test_appstate_exposes_the_four_round4_stores / test_round4_stores_ride_the_same_shared_kv / test_appstate_exposes_the_four_round4_services will NOT break from adding a new funnel_counters store to state.py's _wire() — these tests check specific named attributes (isinstance checks), not an exhaustive attribute enumeration of AppState.

**Conflicts:**
- backend/app/engine/poller_manager.py lines ~96-266 (_event_funnel init/build/rebuild/propagate/setter) are the SAME edit region ★a's 'funnel counters' work must touch to add the parallel _funnel_sink mechanism — this is a same-file, same-function conflict with the EXISTING Round-4 event-detection funnel code, not with another Round-7 item, but it is the single highest-risk hot spot in this whole census and deserves explicit sequencing/review attention distinct from any other item's Overview.tsx/CaseTriageHeader.tsx conflicts.
- backend/app/engine/poller.py lines 213-378 (poll_once) similarly already contains the _event_funnel call site at 291-299; ★a's new self._funnel_sink(delta) call must be inserted without disturbing that block or the audit-write tail (~374).
- ★a and ★b independently both propose changes to backend/app/api/routes_metrics.py (new endpoint beside metrics_posture, line 62) and backend/app/engine/metrics.py (new pure aggregation function) for what the plan itself frames as one feature — this is an intra-plan conflict (not cross-item in the usual sense since both are labeled the same ★ feature) that will produce two competing endpoints/functions/test files if not reconciled before backend work starts.
- Item #1's backend edit (compute_metrics(), metrics.py:111-162) and ★a/★b's backend edits (funnel.py, funnel_outcomes_by_severity()/noise_reduction_metrics() also in metrics.py) both touch backend/app/engine/metrics.py — not overlapping line ranges (item #1 edits compute_metrics's return dict; ★a/★b add new standalone functions elsewhere in the file) but both PRs will touch the same file's import block (TERMINAL_CASE_STATUSES for #1; OCSF_SEVERITY_TO_SCORE/severity_band_from_events for ★a/★b) — low risk, sequence to avoid merge noise.

**Risks:**
- The 'band population' work (SEVERITY_BANDS 5-band ladder in engine/funnel.py) introduces a THIRD severity ladder alongside the two the plan already documents as divergent: webui Overview.bandOf (80/60/35/15, 5-band incl. info) and palette.scoreBand (74/48/22, no info band). ★a's Test plan (line 787) proposes cut points aligned to OCSF_SEVERITY_TO_SCORE (0/10->info,30->low,50->medium,75->high,90/100->critical) which is a FOURTH numeric mapping distinct from both existing client ladders and from priority.py's own _band_from_magnitude (70/40 two-cut high/medium/low, backend/app/engine/priority.py:44-51, confirmed by read). None of these four ladders are backend-test-locked against each other today (no test asserts they agree) — if band_population lands without reconciling this, a case could show 'high' on one surface and 'medium' on another with zero backend test catching the discrepancy. Recommend a new backend pytest asserting funnel_band()'s cut points are consistent with priority.py's _band_from_magnitude()/_BAND_HIGH_CUT/_BAND_MEDIUM_CUT where they overlap, or an explicit documented-divergence test (mirroring the existing pattern used for the two webui ladders).
- Fan-out double-count risk the plan itself flags (line 783) — each per-source Poller child bands ONLY its own source's events; verify with a concurrency test extending tests/test_round4_wave6_poller_concurrency.py (which already proves the pipeline-lock/_event_funnel fan-out is race-safe) that two concurrent poll_once() calls across sources never double-record the SAME cycle's funnel counters — this is exactly the class of bug FINDING #5/#6/#7 in that file's docstring were written to catch for the _event_funnel mechanism, so the same adversarial pattern should be replicated for _funnel_sink before landing.
- kv_mutate-based per-key CAS locking (stores/funnel_counters.py mirroring stores/baseline.py, confirmed base.kv_mutate exists at backend/app/stores/base.py:48) needs its own concurrent-write test (asyncio.gather of two record() calls, per ★a's own test plan line 788) — this is new coverage, not a break, but is the correctness-critical piece of the whole counter store and should not be skipped or stubbed.
- No test currently enumerates or bounds the total AppState attribute surface, so the new funnel_counters store/service additions to state.py's _wire()/_build_round7_stores() are safe from an existing-test-breakage standpoint — but there is ALSO no regression lock (analogous to test_round4_stores_survive_a_wire_rebuild) proving the new store survives a _wire() rebuild; the plan should add one mirroring backend/tests/test_round4_wave3_wiring.py:146-160's pattern for the new funnel_counters store.

- Five CaseDetail-adjacent live-mount/static-source test files (CaseDetail.lazyguards, CaseDetail.footer, CaseDetail.campaign, CaseDetail.live, case-detail-close) are exercised by the combined #9a+#10+#11 CaseDetail.tsx rewrite but appear in NONE of their Files-to-touch or Test-plan sections — the full webui vitest run (not just the files each item's author remembers to run) is the only safety net; recommend the orchestrator explicitly run these five by name as part of the integration gate for #9a/#10/#11, not rely on the per-item test plans.
- The `combos[0]` positional-index risk in CaseDetail.footer.test.tsx (item #10's GradingSection landing in the same dialog) is real but currently mitigated by Radix Collapsible's default unmount-on-close behavior — this mitigation is IMPLICIT (depends on GradingSection never using `forceMount` and always defaulting closed); a future edit to GradingSection for persistence-across-collapse (e.g. to avoid losing typed grading text) would silently break this test. Recommend an explicit regression test pinning `combos[0]` stays the disposition select even with the grading section present and expanded.
- OverviewPanel.tsx's exported `riskFactorBarColor` pure function has an ambiguous fate after the Risk-breakdown PanelCard deletion (see corrections) — if silently dropped, OverviewPanel.test.tsx's import statement (`import { OverviewPanel, riskFactorBarColor } from '../OverviewPanel'`) throws at module-collection time, failing the ENTIRE test file (all 3 describes), not just the 2 tests that use the deleted card. This is a bigger blast radius than the plan's phrasing ('update OverviewPanel.test... no standalone risk-breakdown card') suggests.
- AutomationNudge.tsx is touched by item #6 (slim the banner) but its regression test (AutomationNudge.test.tsx) is absent from #6's own Files-to-touch AND Test-plan sections, despite the file existing today with load-bearing assertions on the exact button name and testid the slimming touches.
- The plan's per-item 'Risks' sections occasionally cite a test assertion that does not exist in the current codebase (see corrections #1: metrics-posture.test.tsx / 'Time to first response') — this suggests the per-item plans were written from a partly-remembered or slightly-stale read of the test suite rather than a fresh grep immediately before writing, so other similarly-unverified test claims elsewhere in the 880-line plan (outside this item's specific scope) may warrant the same fresh-grep treatment before implementation begins.

## [cross] Funnel Counter Safety — Item ★a Noise-Reduction funnel BACKEND (durable raw-alert-by-severity counters, poller/ingest sink wiring, KVStore fitness)


**Confirmed:**
- poller.py poll_once() stats dict (engine/poller.py:289-291) = {polled,new,clusters,investigated,candidates,attached,window_events,funnel_routed} — severity never appears; matches the plan's core motivating claim that raw ingested volume by severity is currently irrecoverable.
