- The existing `_event_funnel` hook (Round-4 Wave-4) is an exact structural precedent for the proposed `_funnel_sink`: Poller.__init__ sets `self._event_funnel=None` at poller.py:105; PollerManager inits it at poller_manager.py:103, propagates in `_build_primary` (poller_manager.py:141-151) / `_build_child_for` (171-207), has a dedicated `_propagate_funnel()` at poller_manager.py:260-268, a capture-off-current-primary step in `rebuild()` at poller_manager.py:221-224, and a public `set_event_funnel()` at poller_manager.py:251-258 — every line number the plan cites for this pattern matches real code exactly.
- state.py wiring points are accurate: `_build_round5_stores()` call is literally at state.py:212 (good anchor for a new `_build_round7_stores()`); `self.poller = PollerManager(self)` at state.py:312 and `self._real_ingest_service = IngestService(...)` closes at state.py:317 (plan's "~317" is the right spot to add sink wiring); `rebuild_log_source()` spans state.py:1283-1310 with the `_event_funnel` re-attach at 1307-1310 (plan's "~1291-1308" is the right region).
- constants.py: OCSF_SEVERITY_TO_SCORE is at EXACTLY line 618 = {0:0.0,1:10.0,2:30.0,3:50.0,4:75.0,5:90.0,6:100.0}, matching the plan's proposed funnel_band cut-point test values (10→info,30→low,50→medium,75→high,90/100→critical) verbatim. BASELINE_NS/BASELINE_KEY at constants.py:162-163 (plan said "~162").
- rbac/policy.py grants "metrics": ["view"] in all 6 role blocks (lines 62,129,142,155,169,184); routes_metrics.py already uses `require_permission("metrics","view")` at lines 67/90/113 for 3 existing GETs. The new GET /api/metrics/funnel reusing this permission needs no RBAC change and will trivially satisfy route_auth_coverage.
- KVStore ABC (stores/base.py:240-333) exposes ONLY abstract get/put plus a concrete `.mutate()` convenience (kv_mutate wrapper, base.py:280-291) that keeps a lazily-created per-(namespace,key) asyncio.Lock (`_lock_for`, base.py:264-278) and a `_rev` compare-and-set (base.py:38-121, 8 retries). There is NO native atomic increment op anywhere in the codebase — the plan correctly builds increment-style semantics on top of read-modify-write via kv_mutate rather than assuming a server-side INCR primitive. This answers the task's core question: KVStore supports get/put + CAS-safe mutate, not increment.
- Both backends' self._kv satisfy KVStore genericly with zero migration: EsKVStore (stores/memory.py:51-93) falls back to `f"{namespace}:{key}"` doc ids for any (ns,key) pair outside its small hardcoded whitelist (memory.py:63-77) — this generic fallback is exactly what a day-sharded funnel store (many distinct keys, not the whitelisted singletons) needs, confirmed working as-is; SqlKVStore (stores/sql/repositories.py:407-427) uses a KVRow table keyed on composite (namespace,key) — also needs no migration for a new namespace value.
- Cluster.trigger_reason.severity_max (models.py:244, via Cluster.trigger_reason: TriggerReason|None at models.py:262) and RawEvent.severity:float (models.py ~67) exist exactly as the plan assumes for band_clusters_by_severity/band_events_by_severity.
- engine/priority.py's _scale_for_case/_normalise_severity (priority.py:72-140) and the per-case severity chip severity_band_from_events (priority.py:143-183) are real and exactly as described; engine/metrics.py currently does NOT import engine/priority.py and priority.py has no reverse dependency (only imports config/constants/models/.risk) — adding `from .priority import severity_band_from_events` into metrics.py is safe, and engine/shift_report.py already imports from .priority (shift_report.py:39), establishing precedent for cross-module reuse.
- quality_metrics()'s auto_closed predicate (`decision_by == DecisionBy.AGENT` over terminal cases, metrics.py:277) and escalated predicate (metrics.py:269-275) match the plan's claimed reuse for funnel_outcomes_by_severity's auto_cleared/escalated tallies.
- webui/src/lib/api.ts getMetrics is at EXACTLY line 1012 — the plan's "next to getMetrics (line 1012)" placement for the new getFunnel client is precise.
- engine/reset.py's ResetHost Protocol, ResetScope tiers, and _CASES_KV / _FACTORY_KV pattern (reset.py:114-141) are real; _clear_kv() (reset.py:258-269) does a bare `kv.put(ns,key,{})` with no CAS — confirmed this only works because every existing _CASES_KV entry is a single fixed (namespace,key) document (baseline/campaigns/batch_jobs are all one-doc stores).

**CORRECTIONS:**
- [HIGH — verified via two existing passing tests] The plan's poller.py integration point is broken as worded: `clusters` (assigned at poller.py:342) and `cluster_stats` (assigned at poller.py:345) are LOCAL variables scoped strictly INSIDE `if new_events:` (poller.py:309) — they are simply never assigned when `new_events` is empty. That is the common steady-state 'quiet poll' case AND the two-tier-ingestion case where every matched event is routed to `funnel_events` instead. The plan says to 'compute... clusters_by_band over clusters; read suppressed/ignored from cluster_stats' and fire the funnel sink 'right before the existing audit write' (poller.py:372) — i.e. OUTSIDE and AFTER the `if new_events:` block — which will raise UnboundLocalError whenever that block didn't run. PROOF #1: backend/tests/test_cursor_poller.py::test_realtime_burst_no_duplicate_across_overlapping_windows (lines 101-134) repeatedly calls poll_once() with `new_events==0` (asserts `s["new"]==0` at line 121) and expects it to return normally — this test will start erroring if implemented as written. PROOF #2: backend/tests/test_round4_wave4_wiring.py::test_events_feed_routes_to_funnel_when_enabled (lines 209-239) asserts `stats["clusters"] == 0` at line 234 PRECISELY BECAUSE `cluster_stats` was never computed on that tick (all 5 events routed to the funnel; `new_events` stayed empty) — direct, already-passing-test proof the locals are unset on this exact code path. FIX: initialize `clusters: list = []` and `cluster_stats: dict = {"suppressed": 0, "ignored": 0}` (or equivalent) BEFORE poller.py:309, or gate the funnel-banding call on whether the block actually ran. The same scoping problem also applies to 'resolve own_source scale once': `own_source`/`strategy` (poller.py:340-341) are ALSO inside the same `if new_events:` block, but the funnel's 'ingested' band must cover `new_events + funnel_events` regardless of whether `new_events` is empty — so the source/scale resolution needed for banding must be computed unconditionally (e.g. right after `feeds = self._source_feeds()` at poller.py:222), not reused from the block-scoped copy.
- [MEDIUM] engine/priority.py:96-112 is NOT quite 'the SourceInstance-lookup half' the plan implies should become `severity_scale_for_source(src)`: the demo special-case at priority.py:93-95 (`if source_id == "demo": return _SCALE_OCSF_0_100`) sits ABOVE that range and is keyed on the CASE's source_id string, not on any SourceInstance attribute available inside a `severity_scale_for_source(src: SourceInstance|None)` signature. Factoring only lines 96-112 silently drops this override, so a demo-tagged source resolved via `prefs.source_by_id(...)` could band as `_SCALE_0_10` instead of the intended `_SCALE_OCSF_0_100` — undermining the plan's own stated goal that the funnel axis stays consistent with the per-case severity chip. Impact is likely low in practice (Poller.poll_once/_run are gated off entirely while demo is active — poller.py:391-403, poller_manager.py:342-350 — so a live, non-demo Poller reading connector_id=='demo' is an edge case), but the factoring boundary as described is inaccurate and should either explicitly replicate an `getattr(src,'id',None)=='demo'` check inside the new function or document the narrower scope.
- [MEDIUM — internal plan contradiction] The 'Shared deps' bullet ('engine/reset.py ... the cases/logs reset tier should call funnel_counters.clear() so a reset also drops the durable counters') treats this as a simple drop-in to the existing `_CASES_KV` tuple pattern (reset.py:120-128), but that pattern only works because every entry there is ONE fixed (namespace,key) doc cleared via a bare, non-CAS `kv.put(ns,key,{})` (reset.py:258-269) — whereas FunnelCounterStore is explicitly per-UTC-day SHARDED (an unbounded, open-ended set of `(FUNNEL_NS,'YYYY-MM-DD')` keys). There is no way to enumerate 'every day-doc ever written' without a KVStore scan (confirmed absent — stores/base.py's KVStore ABC exposes only get/put/mutate; EsKVStore and SqlKVStore add nothing else) or a store-maintained date index (not present in the plan's own `_meta` schema, which tracks only `first_recorded_at`). The plan's OWN Risks/Open-questions sections say this explicitly ('KVStore has no scan, so pruning needs explicit dates') — directly contradicting the Shared-deps claim that reset can just call `clear()`. Needs a resolution before implementation: extend `_meta` to track written date-keys so `clear(dates=None)` can truly enumerate, or have reset.py pass an explicit bounded date range and document older shards as unreachable, or scope this out of Round 7.
- [MEDIUM — understated risk] The plan's Risk analysis ('one extra KV write per poll cycle... fully fail-open... can never slow beyond the existing per-cycle audit write') only reasons about the POLL path. But the SAME sink (`self.record_funnel`) is also wired into `IngestService.ingest()`, which is invoked SYNCHRONOUSLY once per inbound HTTP request from `POST /api/ingest/{source_id}` (backend/app/api/routes.py:421-450, specifically `stats = await state.ingest_service.ingest(...)` at line 449) — i.e. once per webhook/syslog/queue delivery, not once per 'cycle'. Under any of the 16 push receivers at real volume, this adds a 2-3-round-trip kv_mutate (get+put+verify-get, stores/base.py:83-121) to the critical path of every delivery, and concurrent deliveries additionally serialize on whatever lock guards 'today's' shared key (a single per-store asyncio.Lock if FunnelCounterStore literally mirrors stores/baseline.py's pattern, or the KVStore's own per-(ns,key) lock via `_lock_for` if `kv.mutate()` is used directly). This is a materially worse and different risk profile than the plan's stated one-write-per-cycle framing and should be called out, with a recommendation to fire-and-forget the ingest-side record_funnel call (e.g. `asyncio.create_task`, never awaited on the response path) rather than awaiting it inline.
- [MINOR] `funnel_outcomes_by_severity`'s proposed `needs_human` tally ('verdict/status NEEDS_HUMAN') is broader than the quality_metrics() predicate it claims to reuse: quality_metrics()'s own `nh`/`needs_human_cases` (metrics.py:267, 284) checks ONLY `c.verdict == Verdict.NEEDS_HUMAN`, never status. If the funnel endpoint additionally ORs in `CaseStatus.NEEDS_HUMAN`, its total can diverge from the `needs_human_cases` figure shown elsewhere (Metrics/Overview) — reintroducing the exact 'two conflicting numbers' problem item #1 exists to eliminate. Recommend using the identical verdict-only predicate, or explicitly documenting/reconciling the difference.
- [MINOR / simplification] PollerManager.rebuild() (poller_manager.py:209-249) already preserves any manager-level attribute (e.g. a hypothetical `self._funnel_sink`) across every rebuild automatically, because `_build_primary()`/`_build_child_for()` read it fresh off `self` each time (lines 150, 202) — the elaborate 'capture off current primary' fallback in rebuild() (lines 221-224) exists ONLY because state.py bootstraps `_event_funnel` via a DIRECT assignment onto `poller._primary` (state.py:358) instead of the manager's own public `set_event_funnel()`. Since the plan proposes wiring the new sink via `self.poller.set_funnel_sink(...)` (the public-setter analogue) right after construction, that manager-level attribute will already survive every future rebuild() without needing the same bootstrap workaround replicated in rebuild_log_source — the plan's proposed re-application there (mirroring _event_funnel's hack) is harmless but not strictly necessary; worth simplifying/clarifying at implementation time.

**Risks:**
- The UnboundLocalError scoping bug (correction/finding A) is a certain regression if implemented as literally worded in the plan, not a hypothetical — it must be fixed (default clusters=[]/cluster_stats={} before the `if new_events:` block) before this item is considered done.
- Fail-open discipline: every new call site (`self._funnel_sink(...)` in poller.py, `self.record_funnel(...)` in ingest.py) must be wrapped in try/except exactly like the existing `_event_funnel` call (poller.py ~296-301: `try: await self._event_funnel(...) except Exception as exc: logger.warning(...)`) — not a bare await that can propagate into poll_once()/ingest()'s caller.
- #3 isolation is architecturally sound as designed: neither `engine/funnel.py` nor `stores/funnel_counters.py` need to import case_manager or handle_clusters, and handle_clusters() itself stays untouched (only its already-computed return value is read from the caller's scope) — contingent on fixing finding A without reaching into handle_clusters's internals.
- The reset/prune story (finding C) should be resolved or explicitly deferred (with a documented limitation) before shipping, so operators aren't led to believe a reset scrubs all historical funnel counters when day-shards before some point may be unreachable without a scan.
- The ingest-path latency/concurrency risk (finding D) is understated in the plan's own Risks section and should be resolved (fire-and-forget recommended) before this lands on any push-receiver-heavy deployment, since it sits on the synchronous critical path of every inbound webhook/syslog/queue delivery, not just the poll cycle.
- Bucket/retention growth (KVStore has no scan) is correctly flagged by the plan itself as an open risk; no new information here beyond confirming the underlying KVStore ABC indeed has zero scan/list capability in both adapters (stores/memory.py, stores/sql/repositories.py) — so any future pruning sweep will need bespoke bookkeeping, not a generic KV feature.

- routes_metrics.py's existing `/metrics/posture` and `/mitre/coverage` routes establish the exact pattern ★a/★b propose to reuse: `require_permission("metrics","view")` (rbac/policy.py:62 and 5 more role blocks — metrics:view is granted to every role, confirmed), `_load_cases`/`_STORE_FETCH_LIMIT=5000`, and a `truncated`/`store_total` honesty marker. No response_model on these either.

- `CaseFeedbackInput` (used unmodified by item #10) is already defined in api.ts:96-105, not types.ts — item #10 correctly claims 'no new type' since this payload shape already exists and is not being changed.
- The webui already has an OpenAPI codegen pipeline: `webui/scripts/gen-types.mjs` dumps `app.openapi()` to committed `webui/openapi.json` then runs `openapi-typescript` into committed `webui/src/lib/api-types.gen.ts`; `webui/scripts/check-types.mjs` is the drift gate (regenerate + `git diff --exit-code`).
- Verified `check:types` is NOT invoked by `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (only `npm run build` runs there) and NOT called from `webui/scripts/gates.mjs` — it is a manual/local-only script today, despite its own docstring calling itself a 'CI + local' gate.
- Verified `api-types.gen.ts` is provably unimported by any application source file (enforced by its own vitest guard, webui/src/lib/__tests__/api-types-gen.test.ts, 'no src/ module imports it yet') — none of Round-7's hand-written code depends on it.
- Verified empirically that response bodies in the committed openapi.json collapse to a bare `{"type":"object"}` even for endpoints with an explicit `response_model=` (checked both `/api/health`→HealthResponse and `/api/cases/{case_id}`→Case; neither model even appears in `components.schemas`, which only lists ~88 request-body/shared models). So no Round-7 response-field addition (Metrics.active_risk_index, Case.severity_band/severity_source/…, FunnelResponse, NoiseReduction) will ever surface with field-level detail in the generated types regardless of whether gen:types is rerun — the hand-maintained types.ts remains the sole and necessary source of truth, as every item's plan already (correctly) assumes.
- PostureResponse (consumed by items #1/#3/#4 via `posture.lifecycle.{mtta_minutes,mttr_minutes,dwell_minutes}` and `posture.quality.auto_closed_cases`) is NOT declared in webui/src/lib/types.ts at all — it lives in webui/src/soc/pages/Metrics.posture.api.ts:121 (`export interface PostureResponse`), with `PostureLifecycle` at :31-33 and `PostureQuality.auto_closed_cases` at :44. Confirmed no Round-7 item mis-targets types.ts for these fields (they only relabel UI, no schema change), so no correction needed there, but it's a real 'insertion point' gotcha for whoever implements #4 if they assume everything metrics-shaped lives in types.ts.
- The frontend `StageState` type (consumed by StageTimeline.tsx:74-96, cited by items #8/#9a/#9b) lives in webui/src/soc/pages/CaseDetail.api.ts:151-158, with `severity_band?: string | null` and `severity_source?: string | null` as two SEPARATE FLAT fields (mirroring the backend StageState model at models.py:910-915) — confirmed at StageTimeline.tsx:76-77 (`state.severity_band`, `state.severity_source === 'source_asserted'`).

**CORRECTIONS:**
- CRITICAL cross-item contract collision on the exact same field name: item #8 (Cases-list polish) proposes `webui/src/lib/types.ts` Case field `severity_source?: { band: string; value: number; source: 'source_asserted' | 'derived'; scale?: string } | null` (a NESTED OBJECT) and a matching backend addition 'backend/app/models.py — Add an additive optional field to class Case (:1104): severity_source: dict | None = None'. This is WRONG on two counts, verified against real code: (1) `Case.severity_source` is NOT new — it already exists at backend/app/models.py:1177 as `severity_source: str | None = None  # "source_asserted" | "derived"`, a plain string tag, so item #8's 'add' is actually a silent REDEFINITION of an existing Pydantic field with an incompatible type (Python class-body semantics: the later `severity_source: dict` annotation would simply overwrite the earlier `str` one, since both live in the same `class Case`); (2) item #9b (provenance primitive), working from the SAME real backend field, correctly types it as a flat optional string and plans `advisory_bands()` to write plain strings like 'source_asserted'/'derived' into it. If item #8's redefinition landed, item #9b's `case.model_copy(update={'severity_source': 'source_asserted', ...})` would raise a Pydantic validation error at response time (assigning str to a dict-typed field) — a genuine runtime break, not just an inconsistency. Item #8's own precedent citation ('mirrors … the stage-state field already consumed at StageTimeline.tsx:77') is itself the disproof: StageTimeline.tsx:76-77 consumes `state.severity_band` and `state.severity_source` as TWO SEPARATE FLAT fields (CaseDetail.api.ts:153-154), never a nested object with `.value` — so item #8 misreads its own cited precedent. Item #8's `caseSeverity(c)` rewrite (`c.severity_source?.value ?? …`) will not compile once `Case.severity_source` is typed `string | null` (per #9b/reality) since `string` has no `.value` property. RECOMMENDATION: item #8 must drop its own severity_source/backend-field proposal entirely and consume item #9b's flat `severity_band`/`severity_source` fields (i.e. `caseSeverityBand(c) = c.severity_band ?? risk_score`-style, exactly as #9b's own file list already specifies) — the two items' 'Files to touch' sections for types.ts and Cases.tsx must be reconciled into ONE edit, not two independently-authored ones, before any implementation starts.
- Feature-scope contract collision between the two 'companion' Noise-Reduction items themselves: ★a (backend) builds `GET /api/metrics/funnel?window_hours=24` → `FunnelResponse` (`stages[]` keyed ingested|clustered|cases|auto_cleared|escalated|needs_human, each `{key,label,source:'counters'|'cases',total,by_severity}`, plus `drops`, `reduction:{overall_pct,correlation_pct,triage_pct}`, `counters:{available,since,incomplete}`, `cases_meta:{truncated,store_total,fetched}`) and a client method it names `api.getFunnel(windowHours)` — yet ★a's own 'Shared deps' section says the UI item 'consumes GET /api/metrics/funnel + the getFunnel client'. ★b (frontend, the actual UI item for this exact feature) instead independently builds `GET /api/metrics/noise-reduction?window_hours=24` → a DIFFERENT shape `NoiseReduction` (`ingested?/clustered?/cases?/outcomes?/reduction_pct?/human_reduction_pct?/partial?/counters_since?/truncated?/store_total?/window_hours?`, no `stages[]` array) and a DIFFERENT client method `api.noiseReduction(windowHours)`. These are not two views of one endpoint — they are two independently-specified endpoints, response shapes, and client-method names for the SAME feature, and ★b's own plan never references ★a's `FunnelResponse`/`getFunnel` at all. A THIRD, yet again different shape is assumed by items #2/#3 (Overview redesign), written earlier in the doc: they expect a hook `useNoiseReduction(hours)` (which neither ★a nor ★b ever builds — ★b's Overview integration calls `api.noiseReduction` directly inside the page's own `Promise.allSettled`, no separate hook file) feeding a component `<NoiseReductionFunnel>` (★b's actual component is named `NoiseFunnel`, not `NoiseReductionFunnel`) over a payload using `available`/`reason` and ARRAY-shaped `ingested_by_severity:[{severity,count}]`/`cases_by_severity:[{severity,count}]` plus a flat `auto_resolved` — none of which match ★b's RECORD-shaped `NoiseSeverityBreakdown` (`{critical,high,medium,low,info}`) or its `outcomes.auto_cleared_fp` naming. This is a load-bearing, three-way mismatch (endpoint path, client method, hook existence, component name, and response shape) inside what the plan presents as one coherent feature; it must be resolved into ONE canonical endpoint/path/method/shape before any of ★a/★b/#2/#3 are built, or two of the three will need to be thrown away and redone.
- Item #8's claim that 'no test references [severity_ai]' should be read alongside the fact that item #9b ALSO deletes the same `severity_ai` column/SortId/comparator on Cases.tsx and ALSO rewrites `caseSeverity` — the two items' 'Files to touch' for Cases.tsx lines 149-159, 369, 384, 884-903 are near-verbatim duplicates of each other (both delete the same dead code and rewrite the same helper, with incompatible final signatures per the finding above). This is not just 'coordinate ownership' (as both items' open questions suggest) — it is the SAME file edit assigned to two agents with two different resulting type contracts; whichever lands second will conflict with or silently revert the first.

**Exact edit points:**

Verified exact line; backend already serializes this field (models.py:1146), so this is a frontend-only, zero-backend-risk addition.

No fixed line given by #9b beyond 'additive'; recommend placing them adjacent to `entity_type`/`source_id` (:1454-1458) since those are the other 'derived/backend-computed, additive, mirrors backend' fields in the same interface.

**Breaking tests:**
- backend/tests/test_round3_wave2_triage.py — item #9b's severity-ladder unification (70/40 → 74/48/22/8 cut points in engine/priority.py) changes existing band assertions: severity_max 8/10 currently asserts 'high', must become 'critical'; OCSF 90 currently asserts something at the old cut, must become 'critical'; urgency risk=50 currently asserts 'medium', must become 'high'. Item #9b's own plan lists this file as needing updates — flagging it here because it's a REAL pre-existing green test that WILL go red the moment priority.py's cut constants change, independent of any types/api work; the types.ts additions ride on top of this backend behavior change.
- Any webui vitest fixture that stubs a `Case` object and asserts on `SeverityBadge`/`caseSeverity` output for Cases.tsx (e.g. a round-6 severity drill-through test) will need re-verification once `caseSeverity`'s return type/logic changes from 'read risk_score, ignore nonexistent .severity' to 'prefer severity_band, fall back to risk_score' — the exact reconciled version depends on resolving the #8/#9b conflict above first, so writing this test against EITHER item's current draft risks having to rewrite it again once reconciled.
- webui/src/lib/__tests__/api-types-gen.test.ts — will NOT break from any Round-7 API/type change (it only checks internal consistency of the two already-committed generated files against each other, not against the live backend), so it is not a signal that anything here is in sync — this is a common misconception worth flagging since none of the 14 plan items reference it or gen:types/check:types anywhere in their test plans.

**Conflicts:**
- webui/src/lib/types.ts `Case` interface (~1418-1488): touched by item #8 (adds nested `severity_source` object — INCOMPATIBLE), item #9b (adds flat `severity_band`/`severity_source`/`impact_band`/`urgency_band`/`priority_level` strings), and item #11 (adds `objection_window_expires_at`). #8 vs #9b is a genuine type-shape collision on the identical field name (see corrections); #11's addition is independent/compatible with either.

- webui/src/lib/types.ts `Metrics` interface (~1850): touched by item #1 (active_risk_index/active_risk_case_count) and potentially by ★a/★b if either lands its Funnel/NoiseReduction types in the same block 'near Metrics' — low risk since these are simple additive appends, but the orchestrator should sequence so both land without one clobbering the other's insertion diff.

**Risks:**
- No Round-7 plan item's file list or test plan mentions `webui/openapi.json`, `webui/src/lib/api-types.gen.ts`, or `npm run gen:types`/`check:types` anywhere, despite ★a and ★b adding brand-new backend routes (`/api/metrics/funnel` and/or `/api/metrics/noise-reduction`). Because `check:types` is not wired into CI (verified) and the vitest guard only checks the two committed generated files against each other (verified), none of this will turn any gate red — but the committed `openapi.json`/`api-types.gen.ts` will silently drift (missing the new route(s) entirely) unless someone explicitly runs `cd webui && npm run gen:types` (needs `backend/.venv`, which the sandbox has per CLAUDE.md) and commits the diff. Recommend doing this ONCE at Round-7 integration time (after the ★a/★b/#9b backend surface is finalized), not per-item, since re-running it mid-flight across many in-progress agents would just churn a large generated file repeatedly.
- Because virtually every backend route in this app (including ones with `response_model=`) serializes to a bare `{"type":"object"}` in the OpenAPI schema (verified for HealthResponse and Case), there is NO automated cross-check that webui's hand-maintained `types.ts`/`api.ts` contracts actually match what the backend returns for any of Round-7's new/changed fields — every item's backend pytest test plan (e.g. #9b's 'assert GET /api/cases populates severity_band/urgency_band', ★a/★b's shape-contract tests) is the ONLY real enforcement mechanism. Any silent mismatch between a backend test's asserted dict keys and the frontend TS interface's field names (e.g. a stray singular/plural or snake/camel mismatch) would not be caught by tsc, build, or the openapi pipeline — only by a human diff-read or an explicit webui vitest that shapes a mock response using the exact backend field names.
- Item #9b's ladder-unification (74/48/22/8, replacing priority.py's 70/40) is a behavior change that ripples into every consumer of `impact_band`/`urgency_band`/`priority_level` display (not just the new Case fields) — since these are now read-time-populated for the FIRST time via the new `advisory_bands()` enrichment on `GET /api/cases`/`GET /api/cases/{id}`, any existing webui code that today falls back to `undefined` for these fields (rendering nothing) will start rendering real, and differently-cut, bands the moment #9b ships — worth a broader grep-for-consumers pass before merging, beyond the two routes and two test files #9b already lists.
- Both new Noise-Reduction-style endpoints (whichever survives ★a/★b reconciliation) and item #9b's per-case `advisory_bands()` enrichment add per-request/per-case CPU work to `GET /api/cases`/`GET /api/cases/{id}` and a new `/api/metrics/*` route; all are documented as fail-open/try-except in their own plans, which is the right mitigation, but the API+types census specifically flags that NONE of these additions have any typed contract test enforcing they degrade gracefully at the TypeScript layer (e.g. a webui component reading `c.severity_band!` without an optional check) — every consuming component must treat these fields as possibly-null/possibly-absent, matching the `?:` optionality proposed, not assume presence.

## [cross] ROUTE AUTH COVERAGE — route_auth_coverage test rules vs. new /api/metrics/{funnel,noise-reduction} endpoints


**Confirmed:**
- test_route_auth_coverage.py (backend/tests/test_route_auth_coverage.py) has exactly two coverage tests relevant here: (1) test_every_api_route_is_auth_covered (lines 77-93) requires EVERY /api route (any method, including GET) to have `require_auth` in its dependant-call graph OR be listed in PUBLIC_API_PATHS; (2) test_state_changing_routes_declare_authz (lines 150-171) requires only NON-GET (POST/PUT/DELETE/PATCH) routes to carry an authZ dependency (require_permission/require_role/require_fresh_auth/require_admin, matched by qualname in _AUTHZ_DEP_QUALNAMES lines 48-53) or an in-body `_enforce(...)` call, OR be on the explicit _AUTHZ_EXEMPT allowlist (bounded <=31 by test_authz_exempt_allowlist_is_minimal_and_disjoint, line 209). GET routes are NEVER checked for authZ (require_permission), only authN.
- Both new endpoints proposed in the plan (`GET /api/metrics/funnel?window_hours=` [item ★a, line 740/753] and `GET /api/metrics/noise-reduction?window_hours=` [item ★b, line 813/826]) are GET-only, so per the real test rules they need ONLY require_auth coverage — which is inherited AUTOMATICALLY, not manually wired: backend/app/main.py's discover_feature_routers() (lines 26-59) auto-discovers every backend/app/api/routes_*.py module (routes_metrics.py matches) and mounts it with `app.include_router(_feature_router, dependencies=[Depends(require_auth)])` (main.py:111). The plan correctly does NOT list main.py as a file to touch for these endpoints.
- require_permission('metrics','view') is used by every existing sibling GET route in the same file (metrics_posture line 62-68, mitre_coverage line 86-91, mitre_coverage_navigator line 109-114 in backend/app/api/routes_metrics.py) — the plan's proposed new endpoints following the identical `_=Depends(require_permission('metrics','view'))` pattern is consistent with existing code, though technically NOT required by route_auth_coverage for a GET route (it's an extra courtesy authZ layer, harmless).
- The plan's claim '`metrics:view` confirmed in rbac/policy.py:62' is ACCURATE: backend/app/rbac/policy.py:62 has `"metrics": ["view"]` in the RESOURCES vocabulary dict, and all 6 default roles (super_admin — wildcard; soc_manager line 129; analyst_tier2 line 142; analyst_tier1 line 155; responder line 169; auditor line 184) explicitly grant `metrics: ['view']` — so 'already granted to every role, no RBAC change' is correct.
- require_permission(resource,action) (backend/app/api/deps.py:388-395) returns an inner closure literally named `_dep`, whose qualname `require_permission.<locals>._dep` in module `app.api.deps` exactly matches `_AUTHZ_DEP_QUALNAMES` (test_route_auth_coverage.py:49) — confirms the mechanism the plan relies on works as designed (moot for these two endpoints since they're GET, but would work if ever needed).
- Supporting symbols the plan cites in routes_metrics.py all exist exactly as described: `_STORE_FETCH_LIMIT = 5000` (line 43), `_load_cases(state)` returning `(cases, total)` (lines 46-59), and `_window_filter` in engine/metrics.py:445. engine/metrics.py also has `compute_metrics` (111), `quality_metrics` (252), `posture_metrics` (497) as cited elsewhere in the plan.
- state.py precedent for wiring a new KV store matches the plan's claim: `_build_round5_stores()` is called at state.py:212 inside `_wire()`, right after `_build_round4_stores()` (line 207) — consistent with ★a's plan to add `_build_round7_stores()` 'next to _build_round5_stores (line 212)'.
- No test file collision: backend/tests/ has no existing test_metrics_funnel_endpoint.py, test_metrics_noise_reduction.py, test_funnel_counters_store.py, or test_engine_funnel.py — all four new test files the plan proposes are genuinely new paths.
- Neither endpoint, if built exactly as specified (GET + require_permission('metrics','view'), module routes_metrics.py), will fail test_every_api_route_is_auth_covered, test_state_changing_routes_declare_authz, or test_authz_exempt_allowlist_is_minimal_and_disjoint — they add zero non-GET routes and touch none of PUBLIC_API_PATHS/PUBLIC_GET_PATHS/_AUTHZ_EXEMPT, so those tests are unaffected and stay green.
- No other item in PLAN_v1.md proposes a new backend endpoint besides these two — grepped the full 879-line plan for POST/PUT/DELETE/PATCH/GET /api and @router.; every other item (e.g. #1, #9, #10, #11) explicitly states 'no new endpoint' / 'no route change' / reuses an existing POST.

**CORRECTIONS:**
- No factual errors found in the route-auth mechanics the plan describes for these two endpoints — the auth/authZ claims are accurate against real code.

**Exact edit points:**
- backend/app/api/routes_metrics.py — both ★a (`GET /metrics/funnel`, after line 126) and ★b (`GET /metrics/noise-reduction`, also 'beside metrics_posture') target the SAME insertion point in the SAME file; only ONE new `@router.get(...)` + gate should actually land there, not both, since they duplicate the same feature.
- backend/app/engine/metrics.py — ★a adds `funnel_outcomes_by_severity(cases, prefs)`; ★b adds `noise_reduction_metrics(cases, ingest_counters, window_hours, store_total)`. Both are new functions computing overlapping severity/outcome tallies over the same case list — pick one canonical function, not both.
- backend/app/main.py — NO edit needed for either endpoint (auto-discovery via discover_feature_routers(), main.py:26-59/110-111, already mounts routes_metrics.py with require_auth).
- backend/app/rbac/policy.py — NO edit needed (metrics:view already exists and is granted to all 6 roles, policy.py:62/129/142/155/169/184).

**Breaking tests:**
- None of the three route_auth_coverage.py tests (test_every_api_route_is_auth_covered, test_state_changing_routes_declare_authz, test_authz_exempt_allowlist_is_minimal_and_disjoint) will break from adding either or both of these GET-only endpoints — verified against the actual test source; no update to those test files is required by this plan area.

**Conflicts:**
- ★a ('Noise-Reduction funnel — BACKEND durable counters + aggregation endpoint', plan lines 729-801) and ★b ('Noise-Reduction funnel — FRONTEND infographic', plan lines 804-869) independently specify TWO DIFFERENT new endpoints for what DECISIONS.md (line 16-19) describes as ONE feature: ★a builds `GET /api/metrics/funnel` backed by `funnel_rollup()`/`funnel_outcomes_by_severity()`; ★b builds `GET /api/metrics/noise-reduction` backed by `noise_reduction_metrics()`, with a DIFFERENT response shape (compare plan line 761 vs line 831). Both target the identical file+insertion-point (backend/app/api/routes_metrics.py, 'beside metrics_posture') and both extend backend/app/engine/metrics.py with a new but overlapping tally function. ★b's own open-questions section (plan line 863) explicitly flags this unresolved naming/shape conflict ('Confirm the counter work-item agrees, or align on /metrics/noise / /metrics/funnel') — it is NOT resolved anywhere else in the plan or DECISIONS.md.
- Item #2+#3 (Overview redesign, plan lines 96/104/126/813) hard-depends on the `★b` naming — it imports/consumes `GET /api/metrics/noise-reduction` + a `useNoiseReduction` hook by name in its own 'Files to touch'/'New contracts' sections. If ★a's `/metrics/funnel` naming is chosen instead as canonical, item #2+#3's Overview.tsx integration code and its typeof-guard/fallback logic referencing `api.noiseReduction` would need rework before landing — this must be decided BEFORE either backend item is built, not left to be reconciled at integration.

**Risks:**
- Auth-wise there is no risk — whichever single endpoint name/shape is chosen, it will pass route_auth_coverage as a plain GET behind the router's inherited require_auth (no code change needed for that), plus the existing require_permission('metrics','view') pattern used identically by its siblings in the same file.
- The real risk is NOT auth coverage but plan hygiene: DECISIONS.md lists exactly one ★ feature, but PLAN_v1.md splits it into two independently-designed agents (★a backend, ★b frontend) each of which independently invented ITS OWN backend endpoint contract rather than ★b purely consuming ★a's. Before implementation starts, a single owner must pick ONE endpoint path + response shape (recommend ★a's `/metrics/funnel`+`funnel_rollup` OR ★b's `/metrics/noise-reduction`+`noise_reduction_metrics`, not both) so only one lands, engine/metrics.py doesn't grow two overlapping severity/outcome tally functions, and item #2+#3's Overview code targets the endpoint that actually ships.
